Reproduction: a seeded sample of 200 directories run twice (jobs 67054, 67055): the two runs are equal on 13 of 13 files per reading and 59 of 59 audit files; each equals the full run (ranks 189 of 189 and 173 of 173, features 984 of 984 and 831 of 831, audit records 200 of 200; the sample holds none of the 13 dropped directories). Pilot values (job 67056): 912 directories in common with the pilot's `non_test` ranks, 912 of 912 identical, 5,106 of 5,106 feature rows equal; the `pool_aligned` comparison against the pilot's `non_test` files (166 of 740 identical) is not like for like and is not a check. Recount of the FILTER step from the feature table: 3,885 examples and 11,380 positives, equal to the job.

**Decision D147 (tactical; the pool rule of the launch rulings, D140, read on the measured keep rate): N = 100 stands.** FILTER K = 20 with the BM25 teacher keeps 3,885 of 5,789 examples (67.11%; repository-clustered 95% interval 64.0 to 70.0%, 991 repositories, 2,000 draws, seed 0) and 11,380 of 35,804 positives. At the pilot's keep rate (59.49%) the pool would be 3,444 (margin +244 over the 3,200 visits of 100 updates, +7.6%); at the measured rate it is 3,885 (margin +685, +21.4%; +505 at the interval's lower end), so F is at least 3,200 and the rule leaves 100 unchanged (floor(F / 32) = 121). Passes over the pool at 100 updates: FILTER 0.82, RAW 0.55, size-matched subsample 0.82 (it draws as many as FILTER keeps): no arm repeats an example, distinct examples seen per arm 3,200 of 3,885 (FILTER, subsample) and 3,200 of 5,789 (RAW), shortfall 0 (`rq2_plan.py plan --filter-size 3885 --pool-examples 5789`). The sensitivity reading `pool_aligned` has F = 3,320 (margin +120, +3.8%), N = 100. The pilot's keep rate lies outside the full set's interval, so the pilot understated the pool. The 1,000-update sensitivity is not met (32,000 visits). Evidence class: measured (counts, clustered interval), arithmetic (margins). Cheapest experiment that could flip it: none for the `non_test` reading; the dense-teacher cell's keep rate is unmeasured and stays outside this decision.

**Dead ends and surprises.** (a) The first yield attempt used the command-line wrapper, which imports numpy; the login node has Python 3.9 and no numpy, so the yield was computed by importing the state-reading module directly and calling the function the wrapper calls (`state_yield.yield_report`). (b) A poll script written into the local notes folder was refused; the script lives in the session scratch space. (c) Background `ssh` from the tool shell failed repeatedly while foreground `ssh` worked; all cluster checks of this task ran in the foreground. (d) The prompt's "62.6% at the 2,214-row mark" is the 2,835-row figure (measured: 62.24% at 2,214 rows). (e) The `pool_aligned` pilot comparison looked like a failure until it was clear the pilot files hold the other reading.

**Files created on account C (nothing deleted).** `$HOME/ril_prep/runs/full_v1/` (3.9 GB: stage outputs, `non_test/` 2.3 GB and `pool_aligned/` 1.7 GB with the example caches, overlap records 2.8 MB, logs), `$HOME/ril_prep/runs/rep200_names.txt` (5.3 KB), `$HOME/ril_prep/runs/rep200_a/` and `rep200_b/` (100 MB each), job logs under `$HOME/ril_prep/logs/` (prep_66544, prep_67054, prep_67055 about 0.6 KB each; pcheck_67056 19 KB; scratch_ls_67067 and scratch_ls_67068 under 1 KB); node-local scratch `/tmp/ril_prep_full_v1_66544` on gnode006 (8.86 GB) and `/tmp/ril_prep_rep200_a_67054`, `/tmp/ril_prep_rep200_b_67055` on gnode002 (213 MB each), lost with the nodes and not removed. Free space on C after: 20 GB of 50 GB.

**Not run / not known.** No partial-fetch pass (not triggered). No dense-teacher ranks on the mined set; the FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small cell's pool is unmeasured. No `resolve` step, registration, preflight or launch. Q68 stays open. `pool_aligned` counts before the drop list were not computed. Peak memory of the training process on the 5.4 GB shared cache is not measured (the run scripts' 30,000 MB request holds it).

## E-FREEZE-RULING-REPORT-3: the freeze ruling applied to the records, and the final report's third pass (RQ1 ladder, latency, unit-level BM25, freeze, threats). Records and writing, 2026-11-12 (decision D149)

Status: done. Nothing is measured and nothing is run: no cluster job, no model call, no GPU. Evidence class: ruling (the lead's, 2026-11-12) applied to stored records; every number of the report prose is read from a stored table or an entry named in a comment beside it. Branch `b/freeze-ruling-report3`.

**Decision D149** (one decision, five parts, with the reasons; evidence class: reasoned from the stored records and the run registrations; no experiment could flip it, the cheapest check is the guard test below). (1) `rq2_evaluation.json` is **frozen at launch**: the 45 RQ2 runs were submitted on it (launch commit `c2a8db6`, cell-table digest `f441e76574a9...6f16f90`, both read from the launch branch's registration and leakage records; the launch entry itself was not on main when this was written, so it is cited by those records and not by an entry id). (2) `rq3_contexts.json` is **frozen**: the contexts of the 377 executable instances are built on it (E-RQ3-CONTEXTS, configuration hash `e2f011c3...8b4d4c`, manifest `9784f5ae...fb7`) and the RQ3 pilot ran on them; any change after 2026-11-12 invalidates the built contexts and the pilot and needs a new decision. (3) `rq1_dev_selection.json` is **not a frozen configuration**: it records what RQ1 development selected on dev and no final run consumes it; it moved to `configs/records/` (new, with a README: settings recorded for provenance, not consumed by final runs). (4) The statement "at most 0.5 points" is corrected to what was measured: at most 0.59 points on dev L2 nDCG@10 (RQ3 contexts against the dev selection) and 0.48 for the RQ2 evaluation against either other setting; dated correction lines are in this file (after E-FREEZE-AND-LATENCY), in `configs/frozen/README.md` and in the records. (5) The rule is written in both directory READMEs: a record enters `configs/frozen/` only when a registered or built artefact depends on it, and `configs/records/` is for provenance. Reasons: a record labelled provisional inside `configs/frozen/` contradicted the rule that provisional settings never enter it; both frozen records have registered or built artefacts depending on them; the dev selection has none. Conditions to revisit: a change of either frozen record is a new record with its own decision, never a rewrite.

**What moved where.** `configs/frozen/` holds `rq2_evaluation.json`, `rq3_contexts.json`, `MANIFEST.json` (two files; `ruling` field added) and the README; `configs/records/` holds `rq1_dev_selection.json` (moved, `frozen_on` renamed `recorded_on`) and its README. In the two frozen records the `provisional` entries are replaced by a `status` block (state, launch commit and cell-table digest; or build entry, configuration hash and context manifest hash) and the earlier ADR and decision labels are kept as `labels_in_consumer_before_ruling`; the correction line is in each record's `sensitivity`. `ril/bench/freeze.py` (`apply_status`, `RECORD_BUILDERS`, `RECORDS_DIR`) writes the same structure; the records were edited by one script from the stored ones, not retyped, and the new manifest digest is pinned in `tests/unit/test_frozen_records.py` (`97d58d05...baa2`). The guard still fails on a changed frozen record, a changed source configuration, a changed RQ2 run configuration or a missing latency row, and does not cover `configs/records/` (new tests `test_status_follows_the_ruling`, `test_records_directory_is_outside_the_guard`; 11 tests in the file pass).

**Report, pass 3.** Filled (all from stored tables and entries, each prose number with a source comment; `scripts/check_report.py`: 0 findings): the RQ1 ladder (tables `rq1_ladder`, `rq1_ladder_pairs`; rungs, intervals, controls; typed graph not a gain over same-file expansion at L2 and L3 with the stacked test and the tests-and-configure result of E-A-OWNER-ITEMS; the recency prior optimistic because selected on the same dev set; the tokenizer, about 5 points alone and about 1 fused at L2 on chunks, +3.0 and -0.3 at unit level; rerank not built, ADR-009 open); the latency column (path sums 18.34, 23.58, 23.99, 24.72 and 26.76 ms as sums of stage p50 values, two machines, BM25 counted twice at rungs four and five, the flask-size offset of 15% (6 microseconds), fusion at 5.04 ms and why it was not optimised); the unit-level BM25 and field-weight reading (no fused L2 nDCG@10 interval above zero; the Recall@100 differences read as exploratory, nine variants against one control and two levels); the freeze paragraph (records by consumer, the ruling); limits (ansible has no dense scores so dev L3 has two repositories; the Verified-six was not read for the ladder); threats T5 (amended), T21 (recency and expansion tuned on dev), T22 (two-machine latency) and T23 (unit-level texts rebuilt for dev only). The ladder fragments are part of the normal build (the `write_all` function of `ril/analysis/report_tables.py`; `--include rq1_ladder` still accepted); the column widths of the three ladder tables were reduced so none overfills the page; the report test lists the three built-but-not-input fragments (the second encoder's two tables and the per-stage latency table).
Not filled: the launch facts of the 45 runs (accounts, seeds, cell-table digest, leakage tests): E-RQ2-LAUNCH is not on main (branch `b/rq2-launch` unmerged), so they are left out, tracked as row 24 of `docs/REPORT_GAPS.md`. Rows 8 and 9 were updated and rows 23 and 24 added.

**Checks.** `scripts/check_report.py`: 0 findings, 19 open slots (the slot for latency narrowed to a rerank row and a dense end-to-end row; the RQ1 reading slot is filled; the stacked-expansion slot narrowed to Verified-six and the third repository). `tectonic`: 19 pages (17 before), no error, no overfull box. `make lint`-equivalent (ruff check and format, mypy on `ril/`) clean; unit and integration tests: 1,715 passed, 10 skipped (torch absent, flask checkout absent), 25 deselected.

**Open slots and what each waits for.** Abstract, contributions and conclusion: E-RQ2-FILTER, E-RQ3-AGENT; filter and subsample contrasts, exploratory arms, longer-schedule cells: E-RQ2-FILTER, E-RQ2-RAW, E-RQ2-CTRL-SUBRAW, E-RQ2-WEIGHT, E-RQ2-CURRICULUM, E-RQ2-SEC-QQ; the one look on the eight repositories and on the 377: E-RQ2-FILTER; audited noise rate (two slots): E-LABEL-AUDIT-SHEET; sibling-noise decision: E-PILOT-NOISE-SIB; agent budget, runs, threats from agent runs, conclusion: E-RQ3-AGENT; power table: E-POWER; latency rerank and dense end-to-end row: E-LAT-STAGES; stacked expansion on Verified-six and the third repository: E-GRAPH-L3; a citation for SWE-bench Verified and the related-work refresh: no entry (a source note and a dated search).

**Dead ends.** (a) A `git stash` run by mistake in the worktree to compare overfull boxes was popped at once; nothing was lost, the stash list is empty. (b) The first ladder column widths overfilled the page by 35, 12 and 1.5 points; fixed in the builder, not by shrinking the font.

## E-RQ3-TRAINED-ARMS: the pipeline from retained RQ2 models to contexts, prompts and an analysis plan for the trained arms of RQ3. Apparatus, 2026-11-12 (decisions D150, D151)

Status: **apparatus built and tested on CPU; nothing trained was encoded, no GPU job was submitted, no model was called, no resolved rate exists.** Evidence class: measured for the cost arithmetic (stored timings and the ledger), reasoned for the two design lines, tested on synthetic inputs for the code (a fake encoder behind the real encoder interface).

**Why now.** RQ2 training is running on accounts A and C. When it ends, the retained mined models (D142) must be encoded against the executable 377 (units, the six-folder store) and turned into contexts and pseudonymised prompts for the trained arms of RQ3 (D113). Everything except the encoding can be built before the models exist, so that no step waits.

**Inventory (what existed, what a trained arm needed).** Existed: the unit records and instance caches of the 377 (E-RQ3-UNITS), the untrained encoder's score files, the context builder and its byte format (`ril/assembly/rq3_contexts.py`, D108), the prompt builder, pseudonymisation and restore (`rq3_prompts.py`, D120), the driver (`run` takes one prepared directory and one arm name per call, so a new arm needs no driver change), the analysis (`rq3_results.analyse` takes any arm list, one primary pair and estimates), the retention manifest (`ril/training/retention.py`) and the sealed-order guard of the final sets (`ril/evaluation/final_sets.py`, D100, D127). Missing: (1) per-instance unit scores of a retained model on the 377: the RQ2 final-sets job encodes the same stores for the same models but writes its scores under a node-local directory and removes it at exit, so no array survives; (2) a context pass that reads a model's scores instead of the stored untrained ones; (3) a plan of jobs and cost against Block 4; (4) a driver arm run for a full instance list (only the 50-instance model check had a job script); (5) an analysis plan that names the trained arms.

**What was built** (`ril/evaluation/rq3_trained.py`, `ril/assembly/rq3_trained_contexts.py`, `ril/agent/providers/trained_arms.py`, `scripts/rq3_trained_arm.py`, `scripts/ada/rq3_trained_{encode,contexts,prepare,dryrun}.sbatch`, `scripts/ada/rq3_driver/arm_run.sbatch`; tests `tests/unit/test_rq3_trained_arms.py`, 25 tests).
- *Encode stage* (GPU, one model per job): checks the model folder against its manifest, refuses a run the dev analysis does not cover and refuses to start without the id of the finished dev analysis (the same record and guard as `rq2_final_sets.py`: the scores are scores of the executable 377, which is sealed until the dev analysis), encodes the three stored parts at 512 tokens (`encode_store`, `score_units` of the final-sets path), keeps the 377 score files only (the stored part of 36 earlier instances holds more), writes a model record (folder, manifest SHA-256, configuration hash, training commit, CUDA gate, id of the dev analysis, seconds) and a DONE marker; resumable; a dry-run mode that takes the dev set only and refuses a final set.
- *Contexts* (CPU): `build_instance` unchanged (the same assembly, budgets 8,000 and 16,000, cap 1,000, 200 candidates, fixed-header gzip); a *target* is the untrained arms (stored scores) or one retained model (its score file); a pass checks out and parses each instance once for all targets and writes one record directory per model; resumable per (model, instance); a trained arm is the model's scores alone (the shape of the untrained `dense` arm); a build does not need the stored untrained scores, only the instance caches.
- *Prompts*: the driver's own `prepare` run once per model on that model's contexts with the arm name `tr_<run id>` (pseudonymise, then screen, as for the untrained arms); `prepare_trained` wraps the same two calls for tests.
- *Analysis plan*: arms = the untrained `dense` plus one arm per retained model (every cell and seed its own arm, seeds never averaged silently); one primary pair, RAW at the primary seed against the untrained encoder (the smallest seed with a RAW model, a rule fixed here); estimates with intervals and no test for the other seeds of RAW, FILTER-BM25 and the other cells against the untrained encoder and against RAW of the same seed; `rq3_driver.py analyse --plan FILE` reads it; the tables are those of D113 and D121.
- *Plan-only mode* (`rq3_trained_arm.py plan`): lists the jobs in order with cost per job, cumulative cost and whether the cap still covers them; submits nothing, needs no id, reads manifests only.

**Tests.** (a) *Equality:* the untrained arms built by the new path are equal byte for byte to the old path's record on synthetic inputs (3 of 3), and `verify-untrained` (job option `CHECK=1`) does the same on the cluster against the stored contexts of job 63792 (30 instances drawn with the stored seed; stops the build if one differs). (b) *Determinism:* the same model gives the same context bytes (3 of 3) and the same score arrays on a second encode. (c) A model whose scores equal the untrained encoder's gives prompts and context hashes identical to the untrained `dense` arm for every instance (so only the encoder differs). (d) One parse per instance for two models; a rerun reuses every file. (e) Refusals: no id, an id the record does not match, a run the record does not cover, a model that differs from its manifest, a training-stage set outside a dry run, a final set in a dry run, a member without scores, more than one model per job. (f) The dry run on the fake encoder chains encode, score, retrieve, assemble, pseudonymise (an address in a unit text is replaced) and restore, and the text before the excerpts is identical across the trained and untrained arm. (g) Plan and analysis on synthetic rows: one primary pair, no duplicates, the analysis runs with trained arm names. Gates on the merge commit are in the session record.

**Cost against the cap (measured parts, planning constant).** Block 4 cap 6.0 GPU-hours, 0.377 spent (E-RQ3-UNITS), so 5.623 are left. One model costs 0.68 (the brief's constant: 0.377 measured for the 341 built instances plus about 0.29 projected for the 36 earlier ones, as in `rq2f_final_sets.sbatch`). **Eight models fit (5.44, total 5.817); the ninth would pass the cap.** All 15 mined models of the confirmatory and exploratory cells cost 10.20 (total 10.58, over by 4.58); with the conditional FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small cell, 18 models cost 12.24 (total 12.62, over by 6.62). The order decides which models the cap covers: `protocol` (seed, then the seed job's arm order of D141) gives seed 100 in full and three of seed 101; `primary_first` gives RAW and FILTER-BM25 at all three seeds (6 models, 4.08) and two more. Both are printed by `plan`. The encode itself is repeated work: the final-sets job of Block 3 (budgeted at 12.24 for the 377 over 18 models) already encodes the same stores with the same models and discards the scores. Keeping them there would give the trained arms their scores at no extra GPU-hours, at the price of touching the sealed final-sets apparatus after the launch commit: open line Q72.

**Order, commands and wall time (for the lead; the script is `sessions/engineer/rq3_trained_arms_1.sh`, one stage per call, account C).** What must be true first: the RQ2 training jobs of the account have finished (one GPU job per account, D141); the dev analysis is finished and its record is on the account; the retained model hashes are recorded (`plan` prints them from the manifests). Then: `sync` (code, subset, held-out file, commit), `plan` (jobs and cost; decide the order and how many models), `encode` (one GPU job per model, run one after another on the account, about 0.7 GPU-hours each plus the queue wait; 8 models about 5.5 GPU-hours), `contexts` (one CPU job for every model with finished scores, about 2 hours on 4 CPUs for the 377, the parse is done once for all models; `CHECK=1` first rebuilds 30 stored instances and compares bytes where the stored contexts are staged), `prepare` (one CPU job, about 12 seconds per model), then the graded model check and the graded runs under the spend guard of D114 (`rq3_driver/model_check.sbatch` for the 50-instance check, `rq3_driver/arm_run.sbatch` for an arm and run index on the 327 confirmatory instances; both make paid calls and are printed by `check`, never submitted by the script), then `analysis-plan` and `rq3_driver.py analyse --plan`. The prompt, driver and executor environments are on account A, the models and caches on C: the prepared directory of each model is copied from C to A (a copy, nothing deleted).

**Dry run on the cluster (CPU, dev set, no final set, 0 GPU-hours).** Retained smoke model on account A (a 16-dimension hashing encoder, 6 updates, 18.6 kB, manifest present): job 73700 (`rq3_trained_dryrun.sbatch`) loads it, encodes the dev stores on the CPU, scores five dev instances of conan, builds their contexts through the trained-arm path (node-local checkout at the base commit) and prepares pseudonymised prompts and a restore. **Result: not available when this entry was written.** The job (2 CPUs after a request change, 16 GB, 2 h) stayed pending on `QOSMaxCpuPerUserLimit` for the whole session because the RQ2 injected-noise job holds the account's ten CPUs; nothing was cancelled or moved. The same chain was run on a fake encoder in the unit tests (tests above), which proves the code path and not the real checkpoint load, the node-local checkout at the base commit, or the encode of the real dev store. The job's report file and a ledger row record the result when it runs.

**Open lines (design choices not made here).** D150 (provisional: ADR-007): one arm per retained model; the trained arm is the model's scores alone, not RRF with BM25 (Q74: RQ2's evaluation reports both; RRF would double the prompts and the paid calls per model); the primary seed is the smallest seed with a RAW model (Q73 also asks whether RQ3 uses one seed per arm or all). D151: the cap finding (Q72) and the order (Q73) are the lead's. Nothing here enters `configs/frozen/`.

**Dead ends.** (a) A first version located instance inputs with the stored-scores lookup (cache and stored untrained dense file both required); account C holds the instance caches but not those scores, so a trained build would have failed there; trained targets now need only the cache (`locate_cache`, tested with the stored scores deleted). (b) A dry run on the executable 377 would have read the sealed set before the dev analysis; the dry run uses the dev set and the code refuses a final set in a dry run. (c) The brief's single encode-and-context job per model was split in two job scripts: the checkout and parse of the 377 (1 h 50 min on 4 workers in E-RQ3-CONTEXTS) would hold a GPU idle and be repeated for every model; the GPU job encodes only, one CPU job builds the contexts of all models in one pass.

**Not done / not known.** No retained model of a result run was encoded (none exists yet); the real encode time of the six-folder store with a trained model (0.68 is a constant, measured parts only); memory of the encode job (16 GB as the final-sets job; check `MaxRSS` after the first); whether account C has network access from compute nodes for the ten repository clones (the first context build cloned on the node on account A); the 16,000-token prompts of the trained arms (the prepare job takes `BUDGET`, not run); the graded runs.

Files: `ril/evaluation/rq3_trained.py`, `ril/assembly/rq3_trained_contexts.py`, `ril/agent/providers/trained_arms.py`, `scripts/rq3_trained_arm.py`, `scripts/ada/rq3_trained_*.sbatch`, `scripts/ada/rq3_driver/arm_run.sbatch`, `tests/unit/test_rq3_trained_arms.py`, `tests/unit/_rq3_trained_fixtures.py`.


## E-RQ2-LAUNCH: the RQ2 final training jobs launched on accounts A and C. Apparatus and launch record, 2026-11-12 (decisions D153, D154)

Status: **launched; six jobs submitted 2026-11-12 11:13 IST on the launch commit c2a8db6 (account C 73578, 73579, 73580; account A 73581, 73582, 73583); seed 100 of each family is training, seeds 101 and 102 wait on it.** Nothing of the final set is analysed. Evidence class: measured (jobs, logs, `sacct`); the finish times are a projection from the measured arm rate. GPU use of the launch stage before the training jobs: 1.58 GPU-hours (ledger sum: teacher measurement 0.23, smoke, repeat and retention check 1.35, failed starts 0); the training jobs add 34.34 GPU-hours when complete (cell table, the SweRank cell outside it), against the Block 3 cap of 46.7.

**Dense-teacher measurement and the SweRank rule (D153).** Job 71759 (C, gnode070, 13 m 54 s, MaxRSS 6.98 GB, SweRankEmbed-Small at 1,024 tokens, batch 64, seed 0) encoded 100 seeded mined directories (85 repositories, 229,581 function rows, 160,358 distinct texts) in 620.6 s: 0.00384 s per distinct text, 0.0408 s per query. Projected over the 5,789 prepared directories (2,263,190 distinct texts, 14,913,533 function rows): **2.48 GPU-hours with per-repository de-duplication**; the per-directory bound without it is 9.98 GPU-hours, reported beside it. The D125 rule (cell runs only if the projected full-store cost is at most 6.0 GPU-hours) is met on the de-duplicated projection, so **the FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small cell is on**. It is not part of this launch: it runs after the confirmatory arms as separate single-arm jobs, under its own authorization (the lead's authorization covers the 34.34 GPU-hours of the cell table only). Two earlier submissions (71752 gnode057, 71755 gnode060) stopped in seconds at the CUDA gate (the node's GPU is visible to `nvidia-smi` but not to torch); the nodes are excluded in the launch jobs (`SBATCH_EXCLUDE` gnode004, 044, 057, 060).

**F and N.** `scripts/rq2_plan.py resolve` on the prepared store (summary SHA-256 `83d762b9...cb13`): F = 3,885 (FILTER pool after the held-out and overlap drops, `non_test` reading), N = min(100, floor(3,885 / 32)) = **100** updates, 3,200 visits per arm. DEV-041 is filled: not triggered, nothing departs from D68. Distinct-example check in the preflight: RAW 0.55 passes, FILTER 0.82 passes. 45 resolved configurations written (`results/rq2_launch/resolve_summary.json`, `rq2_updates.json`).

**GPU smoke (seed 99, outside the registered set, excluded from every analysis).** Mined RAW on C, 5 updates: job 72149, 22 m 21 s, MaxRSS **11.2 GB against the 30,000 MB request** (the projection was 26 GB; the request stays, it is the largest that can start, D135). Injected RAW on A: job 72150, 19 m 30 s, MaxRSS 7.6 GB. Both started through the CUDA gate and wrote the run outputs and the per-query dev file in the converter's format. Two earlier smoke starts (72143, 72144) failed in 1 s on stale scripts on the cluster copy (a mangled rsync target on my side; no GPU use).

**Retention round trip (D154).** The first in-job comparison of the retained folder with the in-memory model read 3.2e-3 maximum absolute difference. Cause: the run batch removes the checkpoint folder after evaluation, so the comparison loaded the base model; it measured retained-model-against-base, not a retention fault. A repeat (72490, 21 m 25 s, MaxRSS 12.2 GB) confirmed the folder had no weights at that point. The check was replaced by a dev re-scoring of the retained model against the run's own query rows (`scripts/rq2_retention_compare.py`, `scripts/ada/rq2f_retention_check.sbatch`): job 73022 (C, 17 m 11 s) **508 of 508 dense rows equal, maximum absolute difference 0.0**. The first script run failed on an empty cell in the rows and was fixed and re-run on the stored rows (comparison record kept next to the job's output on account C). The real jobs retain every mined arm's model under `retained_final/` on C (three of five present at 12:13).

**Registration.** Cell-table digest `f441e76574a90bf199ee5d634a64fd41904a76505dad044e33f06ce9e1f16f90` on both accounts (`results/rq2_launch/registration.json`); leakage and torch tests 41 of 41 on the launch commit on both accounts (jobs 73496 and 73539; `leakage_ok_A.json`, `leakage_ok_C.json`); preflight `ready` on both (`preflight_A.json`, `preflight_C.json`, same commit, N = 100). All items of the launch checklist (plan section 11.5) are marked done or "not a launch item".

**First 30 minutes and now.** Both seed-100 jobs started within 5 minutes of submission (C 11:13:00, A 11:16:21), logged training steps (the scheduler warning of the first optimizer step is in both logs), and showed **no CUDA, out-of-memory or traceback line** at the 12:13 check. At that point C had finished its RAW, FILTER-BM25 and subsample arms (11:38, 12:02, 12:07; MaxRSS 12.8 GB) and A its RAW, FILTER-BM25 and oracle arms (11:39, 12:02, 12:07; MaxRSS 7.8 GB). Nothing was cancelled, altered or resubmitted after the submission.

**Expected finish (projection, not measured).** Measured rate: about 25 minutes per full arm on C and A early in the order. Seed job on C about 1.7 h, three chained seeds about 5 h; on A about 2.5 h per seed, about 7.5 h for three. With no queue wait between chained jobs: C about 16:30 and A about 19:00 IST on 2026-11-12. At the p90 queue wait (2.75 h per chained job, account A's record; C has no GPU wait history): about 22:00 for C and 00:30 on 2026-11-13 for A. Time limits are 10 h (C) and 18 h (A) per job.

**Not done.** The SweRank cell (3 single-arm jobs, projected 2.48 GPU-hours plus training, needs its own authorization); the dev analysis and its record; the sealed final-sets scoring; any resolved-rate or retrieval number of the final set. Other jobs on the two queues (a CPU dry run of the trained-arm pipeline on A, an interactive request on C) belong to other work and were not touched.

**Dead ends.** (a) Two nodes with a broken CUDA device (gnode057, gnode060) cost two teacher submissions. (b) A zsh expansion mangled the rsync target (`$a:ril_rq2f`), so the first smoke ran stale scripts. (c) The retention comparison was void for the reason above; a failed first re-run (73022 before the fix, empty cell in the rows) is the same check, not a second experiment.

**Files created on the cluster accounts (nothing deleted).** Account C: `~/ril_rq2f/` (scripts, resolved configurations, `work/`, `out/`, `logs/`, `results/rq2/rq2f_mined_*`, `retained_smoke/`, `retained_final/`, `preflight_C.json`). Account A: `~/ril_rq2f/` (same layout for the injected family, `preflight_A.json`). Check lines: `ssh ada 'squeue -u $USER; sacct -X -n -o JobID,State,Elapsed -j 73581,73582,73583'` and `ssh adac 'squeue -u $USER; sacct -X -n -o JobID,State,Elapsed -j 73578,73579,73580'`.

## E-RQ3-SCORE-RETENTION: keep the executable-377 scores of the sealed scoring job for the agent study, and make the trained arms fuse with BM25 like the untrained arm. Apparatus, 2026-11-12 (decision D152, DEV-042)

Status: **apparatus built and tested on CPU (items 1 to 3); nothing was encoded, no GPU job was submitted, no model was called, no resolved rate exists.** Item 1 was merged to the main line first (2026-11-12, before the sealed scoring is submitted); items 2 and 3 follow. Evidence class: measured for the cost arithmetic (stored timings and the ledger), reasoned for the design lines, tested on synthetic inputs for the code (a fake scorer that writes the same files as the real one).

**Item 1, what changed in the sealed scoring job.** `ril/evaluation/final_sets.py` (`evaluate_retained`) and `scripts/rq2_final_sets.py run`: after a part's metrics are computed and written, the per-instance score files of the executable 377 (key `dense_L2`; the stored part of 36 earlier instances holds more, those are dropped) are copied to `<retained root>/exec377_scores/<run id>/dense_scores/` by `ril/evaluation/score_retention.py`, with a record file (checkpoint hash and configuration hash of the model, dev-analysis id and record hash, one SHA-256 per file, a digest of the set, `complete` and the missing ids) and one file per stored part. The scoring row of each part gains a `retained_scores` entry (digest of the part, digest of the set, hash of the record file). On by default for the executable 377 only (`--no-retain-scores` turns it off); the eight repositories are not kept. A file without the dense key is not kept and an instance found in two parts keeps the first part's file, as the agent study's encode stage does. The scores root has no model manifest per folder, so the retained-model listing does not see it (the check is by that file name).

**Tests** (`tests/unit/test_score_retention.py`, 8 tests): metrics byte-identical with and without retention (the result file and the two metric tables of the three parts; the scoring row differs by the retained-scores entry only); only the 377 are kept, as byte copies, with the checkpoint hash and the record hash in the row; first part wins and a file without the key is not kept; the eight repositories keep nothing and retention without the ids is refused; a damaged file or another checkpoint makes a model's scores unusable; the scores root is not read as a model; the command line keeps scores by default for the executable 377 only and still refuses without the id before anything is written.

**Why (Q72 answered).** The job encodes the same 377 instances for the same models and used to delete the scores at exit. Re-encoding them for RQ3 costs 0.68 GPU-hours per model (a planning constant), more than Block 4 can hold for all models (15 mined models of the five confirmed cells: 10.2 against 5.623 left). Retaining them costs no GPU time and reveals nothing the sealed scoring has not already produced; the sealed order is unchanged. **Cost to change my mind:** the copied files add disk on the account that scores (upper bound about 108 MB per model, under 2 GB for 18; to be measured at the first job); if they do not fit, `--no-retain-scores` and the encode fallback apply.

**Item 2, the trained arm fuses with BM25 (Q74 answered).** The frozen RQ3 context configuration is RRF of BM25 and dense (k 100, 512 tokens), assembled by D102 and D103. As built by E-RQ3-TRAINED-ARMS the trained arm used the model's scores alone (D150); that changed the fusion structure as well as the retriever, so it is changed: `ril/assembly/rq3_trained_contexts.py` (`trained_arm(name, rrf_k)`) is now `rrf_arm_score` over the stored BM25 scores and the model's `dense_L2`, with the same signals and the spec's constant, and the constant has no default so that a build cannot use another one. The scores are read by `rq3_trained.resolve_scores`: the retained scores when they verify (record names the model's checkpoint hash, complete for the 377, every file hashes to the record), else the encode stage's output, else a refusal that names both places; `scripts/rq3_trained_arm.py contexts` takes `--retained-scores` and `--retained-root` and writes a source record per model next to its contexts (source, directory, checkpoint hash, set digest, rrf k). The analysis plan's comparator is the untrained `rrf` arm (`ta.UNTRAINED_ARM`), so the primary pair is RAW at the smallest RAW seed in the fused form against the untrained encoder in the same fused form; the driver reads the plan file unchanged (`rq3_driver.py analyse --plan`). **Equality test:** a model whose dense scores equal the untrained encoder's reproduces the untrained `rrf` arm's context records (arm name aside) and prompts byte for byte, for 3 of 3 instances at both budgets; the old test of the dense-only form was replaced by this one. A build of the old dense-only form is rebuilt, not reused, because the arm's name and signals are part of the build's hash.

**Item 3, plan-only output and the order (Q72 to Q74 answered).** `rq3_trained_arm.py plan` reads `<retained root>/exec377_scores` (or `--retained-scores`): a model with verified retained scores costs 0 GPU-hours and is listed with `scores: retained`; the others are listed in the order of Q73 (`pair_seed100`, now the default: RAW and FILTER-BM25 at all three seeds, then WEIGHT and CURRICULUM at seed 100, then their other seeds, then any other cell, which the rule does not name, last) with the cost, the running total and `fits`, and the list stops at the first model that would pass the cap. The rule is printed in the output (`order_rule`). `protocol` and `primary_first` remain selectable. The table `results/rq3_trained/block4_plan_18_models.csv` is rebuilt for the three orders (fallback, no retained scores). The analysis plan referred to the untrained `dense` arm and now refers to `rrf` (item 2); nothing else in the driver's analysis plan referred to trained scores alone.

**Revised cost against the cap (planning constant 0.68 GPU-hours per model, measured parts only).** With retention, every retained model costs 0 in Block 4 and the 5.623 GPU-hours left stay free. Fallback, per model without verified scores: the primary contrast (RAW and FILTER-BM25, three seeds, 6 models) 4.08 (Block 4 total 4.457); plus WEIGHT and CURRICULUM at seed 100, 8 models, 5.44 (total 5.817 of 6.0); the ninth would pass the cap. All 15 mined models of the five confirmed cells would cost 10.2 (total 10.577, over by 4.577), 18 models 12.24 (over by 6.617): retention is what makes the full set affordable. The same figures as the earlier entry for the cost per model; nothing new was measured.

**Tests** (`tests/unit/test_rq3_trained_fusion_retained.py`, 10 tests; `test_rq3_trained_arms.py` updated to the fused form, 25 tests; `test_score_retention.py`, 8 tests): the arm equals the untrained `rrf` arm's score function and signals and uses BM25 (a dense-only arm does not); the equality test above; the fused arm's record carries the build's hash; resolve prefers verified retained scores, refuses another checkpoint, an incomplete set and a damaged file, and falls back to the encode output with the reason; the plan costs retained models 0 and charges only the rest; the Q73 order for 18 models (primary pair first at all seeds, then seed 100 of WEIGHT and CURRICULUM) and its cap stop at eight; `contexts` builds from retained scores for one model and from the encode output for another, with the fused arm at the spec's constant, and writes the source; a model with neither is refused; `plan` and `analysis-plan` from retained records.

**Commands for the lead (updated; `sessions/engineer/rq3_trained_arms_1.sh`).** `plan` now passes the retained scores and the order `pair_seed100`; `contexts` passes `RETAIN_DIR` so the retained scores are read; `encode` is for models the plan lists as `scores=encode`; the analysis plan takes `--retained-root` and `--retained-scores`. The sealed scoring submission needs no new command line: it keeps the scores by default (commit `20c3ab1` or later as `RIL_COMMIT`).

**Considered, cautions (no dead end was hit; nothing was tried and abandoned).** (a) Keeping the score files inside each model's own folder was considered and not done, so that the model folder, which the archive and copy scripts move as a unit and whose digest the manifest records, stays exactly what its manifest describes; the scores are kept beside the models in their own folder, which has no model manifest and so is not read by the retained-model listing (tested). (b) The final-sets scoring writes scores per stored part into node-local space; retention copies per part, so a rerun that skips a finished part cannot copy that part's scores (they are gone with the node); a part is therefore marked finished only after its copy, and an incomplete record (`complete: false`, the missing ids listed) is not used by the context pass, which falls back to the encode stage. (c) The stored part of 36 earlier instances holds more instances than the subset; they are dropped at the copy, and an instance found in two parts keeps the first part's file, as the encode stage does.

**Not done / not known.** The size of the kept scores (upper bound only); that the real job writes the files where `part_scores_dir` looks (derived from `eval_checkpoint`'s default dense directory and tested with a stand-in; the first real job is the check, and the rows say `complete`); the dry run of the trained-arm path on account A (job 73700, pending when E-RQ3-TRAINED-ARMS was written) was submitted with the earlier code, so it exercises the dense-only form and the new code has not run on the cluster; no model was encoded; no resolved rate exists.

Files: `ril/evaluation/score_retention.py`, `ril/evaluation/final_sets.py`, `ril/evaluation/rq3_trained.py`, `ril/assembly/rq3_trained_contexts.py`, `ril/agent/providers/trained_arms.py`, `scripts/rq2_final_sets.py`, `scripts/rq3_trained_arm.py`, `scripts/ada/rq2f_final_sets.sbatch`, `scripts/ada/rq3_trained_contexts.sbatch`, `scripts/ada/rq3_trained_encode.sbatch`, `tests/unit/test_score_retention.py`, `tests/unit/test_rq3_trained_fusion_retained.py`, `tests/unit/test_rq3_trained_arms.py`, `results/rq3_trained/`.

## E-RQ3-MODEL-CHECK-PLAN: the model check extended to two providers, with the selection rule fixed before any call. Apparatus and plan, 2026-11-12 (decision D155)

Status: **apparatus built and tested on scripted fixtures; no model was called, no network call to any provider was made, nothing was spent, no price, rate limit or resolved rate exists.** Evidence class: reasoned (the rule); exploratory when it runs (the 50 held-out instances are excluded from the confirmatory analysis, D111). Cheapest experiment that could flip D155: the check itself.

**Why.** The model is a fixed instrument in a paired comparison of retrievers; it needs enough resolved instances for power, a cost the study can repeat k times for every arm, reliable edit format and acceptable data terms. D112 chose cheapest-first among one provider's models. D155 widens the list to two providers and makes capability the first criterion with cost as a constraint (text and pushback in `docs/RQ3_PILOT.md`, "Model check across providers").

**Decision D155 (one decision, written before the code).** Candidates `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, `gemini-3.8-flash` and two models of a second provider (strings in the lead's local limits file), closed. Rule: discard a candidate whose projected whole-study cost exceeds B (default 150 USD, from the limits file) or whose resolved fraction on the 50 is below 15%; take the highest resolved count of the rest; within 2 instances of it the cheaper; none passing means no choice. Same instrument for all (prompt, edit format, temperature 0, two follow-ups, pseudonymise-and-restore, spend guard), thinking pinned per candidate at the provider's lowest fixed setting the scaffold works with.

**What was built.** (1) `ril/agent/providers/messages_api.py`: client for a messages-style chat API behind the same `generate(contents)` interface and `CallResult` as the Gemini client (endpoint, header names, version header and key variable are configuration in the model's limits entry, so the tracked source is provider-neutral; the key comes from that variable or its line in the key file, masked and scrubbed; persistent limiter; 429, 5xx and 529 retried with backoff; usage as input, output including thinking, thinking tokens separately only where the API reports them, otherwise 0). (2) `InsufficientBalance` (HTTP 402 or the credit-balance message): a hard stop in both adapters, ends the run cleanly with `stopped: insufficient_balance` and a resumable log. (3) `load_api_key(variable=...)`: one key file, one line per provider. (4) `ril/agent/providers/model_check.py`: limits-file schema and validation (refuses any UNSET value, fewer than two providers, a messages-style model without its connection fields, non-ascending caps), per-candidate summary (counts, Wilson interval, tokens, realised and projected cost), the rule, the table, and the power lookup. (5) `scripts/rq3_driver.py`: `run` takes the provider, price (the limits entry; `prices.yaml` stays the fallback) and thinking from the limits entry, records `provider` and `config_sha256` in `meta.json`; new `model-check-limits` (validate, no key, no call) and `model-check-select` (the rule on `RUNS_DIR/<candidate>/`; exit 3 when nothing is selected or a candidate is unfinished). The D112 `model-check` command and `choose` stay for the record. (6) `configs/agent/model_check_limits.template.yaml` (unfilled). (7) Four numbered scripts for the lead in `sessions/engineer/rq3_model_check_multi_{1_keys,2_limits,3_run,4_select}.sh`.

**Tests (scripted, no network).** `tests/unit/test_providers_messages.py` (17): request bytes (no seed, `thinking` only when set, follow-ups as assistant turns), usage with cache and thinking tokens, refusal and malformed replies, retries on 429, 529 and a network fault, retries exhausted, 401 not retried, 402 and the credit message as a hard stop, the key never in url, body or error text, key lookup per provider, **equivalence: both adapters give `run_units` the same row keys and values for tokens, attempt and final, and `reply_from_rows` the same reply**, the spend guard stopping the run on the second provider's usage, resume after a hard stop. `tests/unit/test_model_check_multi.py` (16): template refused until filled, two providers and connection fields required, budget default 150, price from the limits entry, provider choice and config hash, projection arithmetic, incomplete candidates, the rule (clear winner, near tie to the cheaper, budget before capability with equality passing, floor inclusive at 15%, nothing chosen, ties by name), and the commands on stored rows. Whole fast suite: 1,772 passed; ruff and `mypy --strict` clean.

**Commands for the lead, in order** (all under `sessions/engineer/`; none runs by itself except what its name says; steps 3 makes paid calls under the guard):
1. Enable billing with a hard cap on each provider project; read the billed-tier data terms.
2. `bash sessions/engineer/rq3_model_check_multi_1_keys.sh gemini`, then `... messages` (hidden prompt on the cluster, mode 600, one line per provider).
3. `cp configs/agent/model_check_limits.template.yaml sessions/engineer/model_check_limits.yaml`; fill every UNSET from the providers' own consoles, pricing pages and API documentation (dates and page names included; for the second provider also the endpoint, key variable, key header and version header, and replace the two placeholder model keys by the model strings of D155); then `bash sessions/engineer/rq3_model_check_multi_2_limits.sh` (validates, copies).
4. `PREPARED=ril_rq3_driver/out/prepared_b8000_65086 bash sessions/engineer/rq3_model_check_multi_3_run.sh` (tier 1; the four candidates are chained one after another; resumable). A candidate that stops on its cap (`"stopped": "spend_cap"` in its `meta.json`) is re-run with `TIER=2 ONLY=<model>`.
5. `bash sessions/engineer/rq3_model_check_multi_4_select.sh` (copies the runs, applies the rule, prints the table and the selected model).

**Expected cost of the whole check.** Not known in dollars: prices are read by the lead at run time and none is stored. Formula from the measured pilot tokens per 50 instances (631,439 input and 46,625 output central; 688,436 and 152,501 in the high scenario that multiplies output by 3 for thinking, an assumption): cost of a candidate = input tokens x input price + output tokens x output price, per million. The cap tiers in the template (1, 3 / 3, 8 / 3, 8 / 5, 15 USD) are placeholders and the lead's to set; they sum to 12 USD at tier 1 and 34 USD at tier 2 and are the upper bound of what the guard allows. The tokens are those of one provider's tokenizer; the other provider's differ, so the first candidate run of each provider is the real measure.

**Provider errors.** Insufficient balance (402 or the credit-balance message): the run ends cleanly, nothing is retried; top up, re-run the same tier, it resumes and the logged cost counts. 401 or 403: key or project wrong, nothing billed, redo step 2. 429 and 5xx: retried by the client with backoff; `provider_unavailable` after five retries leaves the log intact, re-run later at the same tier. Spend cap: re-run at the next tier. `exec_error` rows (environment faults before the patch): re-running the same command re-executes those units from the stored replies; the aggregation refuses a candidate that still has any.

**Public leaderboard context (unverified lead, as the reading rule requires: search snippets read on 2026-11-12, no page read in full, no score quoted).** A search for the four names found a public SWE-bench Verified board (a third-party aggregator running one fixed mini scaffold) listing `gemini-3.8-flash` and `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`; it did not find the second provider's two candidates under their exact strings (a differently named entry of the larger one's family appeared, which is not the candidate). Boards differ in scaffold, reasoning setting and date, and none is comparable to retrieval-supplied one-shot repair on the executable 377; this is context only and decides nothing. The lead should read the boards at run time before the entry is quoted anywhere.

**What the entry cannot yet state (filled after the run, mechanically).** The projected whole-study cost of the chosen model, its base resolved rate and what that means for power: the stored power grid (`results/rq3_driver/rq3_power_run_noise_mdd.csv`, assumed rates 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, N = 327) gives minimum detectable differences of 4.4 to 9.7 points; `model-check-select` prints the row of the nearest grid rate for the chosen model. Observation from the arithmetic only: at the pilot's 12.6k input tokens per unit, 12 arm-runs of 327 is about 50 million input tokens, so the default B of 150 USD will exclude a model whose input price is high enough; which candidates that is depends on the prices read.

**Dead ends / limits.** (a) The second provider's usage object has no separate thinking count as far as the adapter knows, so `thought_tokens` is 0 unless the field appears and the thinking share sits inside `output_tokens`: cost is right, the separate log is not available for that provider. (b) The adapter's request shape (`model`, `max_tokens`, `temperature`, `messages`, `thinking` and the usage and stop fields it reads) is written from the provider's documented API and tested only on a scripted transport; the first real call is the test, so the cheapest candidate of each provider should run first (the script's order puts Gemini first and the smaller second-provider model before the larger). (c) The second provider's API has no model-level seed; run noise is left to the k repeats (D155). (d) Whether a "lowest fixed thinking setting" is accepted with temperature 0 differs by model; if a call is rejected with a 400 the lead sets `thinking` to null for that candidate in the limits file and the config hash records it.

**Not done / not known.** Any call, any price, any rate limit, any resolved rate, the choice of model, the leaderboard reading in full.

Files: `ril/agent/providers/messages_api.py`, `ril/agent/providers/model_check.py`, `ril/agent/providers/{gemini,pilot_run,spend}.py` (hard stop, key variable, price from limits), `scripts/rq3_driver.py`, `configs/agent/model_check_limits.template.yaml`, `tests/unit/test_providers_messages.py`, `tests/unit/test_model_check_multi.py`, `docs/RQ3_PILOT.md`.

## E-RQ3-MODEL-CHECK-AMEND: one provider and one candidate by written key, cost projected with a measured cache-hit fraction, adjacent repeats. Apparatus, 2026-11-12 (decision D158, amends D155)

Status: **code and tests only; no model was called, no network call was made, nothing was spent.** Evidence class: reasoned (the arithmetic below), the probe it adds measures the one number that is assumed nowhere. Cheapest experiment that could flip it: the probe itself (20 calls, well under 0.5 USD).

**Why.** The provider's pricing page (read by the lead on 2026-11-12) lists `gemini-3.8-flash` at 0.75 / 3.75 USD per million input / output tokens, 0.075 for cached input, and the prices double on 2027-01-01. At the measured pilot volume (about 4.13M input and 0.31M output tokens per 327-instance arm-run) one arm-run costs about 4.2 USD uncached, so the 12 arm-runs of the plan cost about 51 USD, above the study budget of 45 and the lead's ceiling of 50 USD. Runs 2 to k of the same arm send the same prompts; a provider that serves repeated prompt prefixes from its cache bills their input at the cached price with unchanged outputs. Whether and how much it does is measured, not assumed. The second provider's billing was unavailable, and the lead dropped `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` (a weaker model should not take study money when the stronger one fits the budget with measured caching).

**Decision D158 (one decision, amending D155).**
1. A single provider is allowed only when the limits file carries `single_provider_ok: true`, a single candidate only with `single_candidate_ok: true` (both must be the literal `true`; absent means the two-provider, several-candidate default). With one candidate the selection reduces to the two filters (projected whole-study cost within B with measured caching, 15% floor): if it fails either, nothing is chosen; no weaker fallback is added. The result states the single-provider limitation (the model is a fixed instrument, the claim concerns the retrievers) and lists the D155 candidates that were not run, "not run, not available at the time"; the selection compares only finished candidates and the lead may add one later and re-run it.
2. Projected whole-study cost: the first run of each arm uncached, runs 2 to k at `input x ((1 - h) x input price + h x cached price)` plus output at the output price, with h the hit fraction measured by the probe; with no probe, or no cached price in the entry, the projection is uncached. `study_runs_per_arm` (default 3) and `study_arm_runs` (12) give 4 arms x k = 3; the file is refused if the second is not a multiple of the first. The table prints both projections and the hit fraction.
3. Probe (`ril/agent/providers/cache_probe.py`, `rq3_driver.py cache-probe`): the first 10 held-out prompts in the fixed (instance, arm) order, each sent twice back to back with an identical request (that of a unit's first call), the provider's reported cached-token count read from usage; hit fraction = cached tokens of the second sends over their prompt tokens; rows hold hashes and counts, no prompt text; it runs once per candidate (a repeat needs `--force`), goes through the same spend guard with its own cap (0.5 USD in the job script), and the check's guard counts the probe's billed rows against the check's cap. A probe stopped by its cap or with fewer than 10 complete pairs is not usable.
4. Adjacent-repeat scheduling (`--run-indices K ... --adjacent-repeats`, `RUN_INDICES` in `arm_run.sbatch`): order (instance, arm, run index) instead of (run index, instance, arm); the set of units, seeds, requests, outputs, hashes and the analysis order are unchanged; `meta.json` records `schedule: adjacent_repeats`. The guard counts the tokens the provider reports as billable (prompt tokens, of which the reported cached tokens at the cached price, and output including thinking); cache hits are never assumed in cost, and a provider that reports none is priced uncached.
5. `gemini-3.6-flash` is refused as a candidate by the validator (previous generation at the same price as `gemini-3.8-flash`).

**Built and tested** (`tests/unit/test_model_check_cache.py`, 16 tests, scripted transport): both written keys needed and literal; the previous-generation model refused; cached price at most the input price; projection arithmetic with and without a fraction (12 uncached arm-runs against 4 x (first + 2 repeats)); a probe fraction makes the one-candidate case pass the budget where the uncached projection fails it; a candidate that fails the floor or the budget is not replaced; cached tokens read from usage and priced at the cached rate, the guard counting the reported tokens; probe request sequence (each prompt twice in a row, identical bytes, 20 requests for 10 prompts), no prompt text in the output, a probe stopped by its cap unusable; the adjacent order and the default order contain the same units, the same request set, equal result rows apart from timing fields and equal call-log hashes; the select command reads the probe file. The existing 54 tests of the providers and driver pass unchanged.

**Not done / not known.** The probe result, any resolved rate, the hit fraction, the realised cost and the choice of model. Prices are read from the limits file, which the lead fills from the provider's own pages; the cached price is a value the lead's reading gave and is dated there.

Files: `ril/agent/providers/{model_check,cache_probe,spend,gemini,pilot_run,driver}.py`, `scripts/rq3_driver.py`, `scripts/ada/rq3_driver/{model_check,arm_run}.sbatch`, `tests/unit/test_model_check_cache.py`.

## E-RQ3-MODEL-CHECK-RESULT: gemini-3.8-flash on the 50 held-out instances, thinking level low. Measured, exploratory, 2026-11-12 to 2026-11-13 (decisions D155, D158, D159)

Status: **done; one candidate, one provider.** Evidence class: measured (config hash `0fe87a17bf1b`, seed 0, temperature 0, arm RRF at 8,000 tokens, run 0, account A, call log and rows under `results/rq3_driver/model_check_think-low/`). The 50 instances are the held-out slice (D111): exploratory, excluded from the confirmatory analysis.

**What ran.** (1) First run, provider-default thinking: probe of 7 pairs (hit fraction 0.626, 0.3775 USD) and 12 of 50 rows (0.611 USD), stopped on the 1 USD cap. About 96% of its output tokens were thinking tokens (3,929 of 4,092 in the first probe row). Kept untouched as a superseded configuration (config hash `4f1dfbe772`); not used for selection (D159). (2) Re-run from scratch with `thinkingConfig.thinkingLevel = "low"`: cache probe, then the 50 instances, first cap tier only, stopped: complete.

**Table (D155 selection run mechanically; `model-check-select`).**

| model | resolved on 50 | rate [95% Wilson] | unusable after follow-ups | arm-run cost (327) | projected study (12 arm-runs) | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| gemini-3.8-flash | 17/50 | 34.0% [22.4%, 47.8%] | 1/50 | 4.35 USD | 36.27 USD (cache hit 75.7%); 52.18 USD uncached | passes budget (45) and floor (15%); selected |

Outcomes: 17 resolved, 22 executed and unresolved, 10 edit sets that did not apply, 1 unusable. Calls: 67 (50 first calls, 17 follow-ups).

**Tokens (all answered calls, thinking level low).** Prompt 594,991; thinking 4,103; answer 54,199 (output total 58,302 tokens; thinking is 7.0% of it). **Truncation at the output limit (4,096 tokens): 8 of 67 calls, 11.9%** (finish reason `MAX_TOKENS`); one instance ended unusable. Thinking at low is no longer the cost driver, the long edit answers are.

**Cache probe (D158).** 9 of 10 planned pairs finished, hit fraction 0.757 (second-send cached tokens over their prompt tokens), billed 0.163 USD. The fraction applies only to repeats of an identical prompt sent back to back (adjacent-repeat scheduling, D158); it comes from 9 pairs and is a point estimate.

**Spend against the authorisation** (total 50, check 5, first tier 1, second tier 3 USD): superseded run 0.988 + re-run 0.702 (probe 0.163, check 0.540) = **1.690 USD**, all on the first tier; the second tier was not used.

**Selected model.** gemini-3.8-flash, the only candidate run. Limitations stated: one provider (the second provider's billing was unavailable), one candidate (the weaker model of D155 was dropped by the lead); `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` and the second provider's models are "not run, not available at the time", and the lead may add one later and re-run the selection. The model is a fixed instrument; the claim concerns the retrievers. 34% on the 50 is not a statement of agent capability.

**What remains.** (a) The graded RQ3 runs: 12 arm-runs, projected 36.27 USD only if the repeats are sent adjacent and the provider serves about 76% of repeated input from cache; without cache hits the projection is 52.18 USD, above B (45) and above the remaining ceiling (50 - 1.69 = 48.31 USD). The credit the lead must have available is therefore 36.27 USD plus a margin (about 37 to 38 USD); the guard stops a run before it passes its cap, so a lower hit rate stops the study early instead of overspending. (b) Open for the lead (design, not decided here): 11.9% of calls end at the 4,096 output limit; the limit is part of the configuration hash (D155), so raising it changes the hash and the cost, and the check would need a repeat. Cheapest experiment: the same 50 with a higher limit, about 0.7 USD, a few minutes. (c) The probe has 9 pairs; if the lead wants a tighter fraction, 20 more pairs cost about 0.4 USD.

**Dead ends.** The default-thinking run: its rows describe a configuration the study will not use; the 0.988 USD it cost is counted against the authorisation. Resuming it on the second tier was not done.

Files: `results/rq3_driver/model_check_think-low/{model_check_multi.json,runs/}`, `results/logs/ada_jobs.csv`.

## E-RQ3-MODEL-CHECK-THINKING: thinking level fixed to low; the first check run kept as a superseded configuration. Apparatus, 2026-11-13 (decision D159, amends D155)

Status: **configuration change and reporting code; the re-run is recorded in E-RQ3-MODEL-CHECK-RESULT.** Evidence class: measured (the superseded run), read in the provider's documentation (field name and levels).

**Why.** The first check run (cache probe of 7 pairs, 12 of 50 rows, stopped on the first cap) left the thinking setting at the provider's default. In the probe rows about 96% of the output tokens were thinking tokens (3,929 of 4,092 in the first row) and several calls ended at the output allowance, so those rows do not describe the configuration the study will use. The rows, the probe and the call log stay on the cluster untouched as a superseded configuration (output directory `check_gemini-3.8-flash_rrf`, spend 0.988 USD of the 5 USD check authorisation, counted below).

**Decision D159.** (1) Thinking is fixed to the lowest level the model accepts, sent as `generationConfig.thinkingConfig.thinkingLevel = "low"` (documentation for the model: levels low, medium, high; minimal is not supported; the level is the field for the 3.x generation, the older token-budget field is not used). It is set in the limits file entry and enters the configuration hash, so the new run has its own hash. (2) A new run uses a new output directory through `RUN_TAG`; nothing of an earlier configuration is overwritten or deleted. (3) The table reports, per candidate, tokens split into prompt, thinking and answer, and the share of answered calls that ended at the output allowance (`finish_reason = MAX_TOKENS`), from the call log (`call_stats`). (4) The caps are those of the limits file; spend of a superseded configuration counts toward the authorisation total, not toward the new run's cap.

Files: `ril/agent/providers/model_check.py`, `scripts/rq3_driver.py`, `scripts/ada/rq3_driver/model_check.sbatch`, `tests/unit/test_model_check_cache.py`.

## E-REPORT-DRAFT-4: power table, citations and related work, consistency proofreading; the report's fourth pass. Writing and reading, 2026-11-12 (decisions D156, D157; open question Q75)

Status: **done; no new result, no model call, no cluster job, no push.** Evidence class: read in full (three paper notes, each PASS in `scripts/check_paper_note.py`), measured (the power rerun reproduces the stored files byte for byte), reasoned (the power inputs). Branch `b/report-pass4`.

**Power (E-POWER).** The run-noise analysis was a stored seeded script with stored outputs, not prose: `ril/analysis/rq3_power_noise.py`, outputs `results/rq3_driver/rq3_power_run_noise*.csv` and the assumptions file, the report table `rq3_power` generated from the mdd file. Rerun (`scripts/rq3_driver.py power --n 327 232 --sims 500 --permutations 2000 --seed 0`, 20 s): the three outputs have the same SHA-256 as the stored ones, so the numbers of the earlier entries (4.4 to 9.7 points minimum detectable at N = 327) are reproduced, not recomputed differently. Added: generated table `rq3_power_small` (power of a 4-point difference, 21 to 75%, no cell at 80%; size of the test 2.4 to 5.0%), `tests/unit/test_rq3_power_reproduction.py` (hashes, settings, the report's range and four-point statement on the rows; a slow rerun test over all N = 327 rows). The slot "power table from graded runs" is replaced by the statement above; the version built from the discordance and run spread of graded runs stays open because no graded run exists. Entry text: `docs/EXPERIMENTS.md` E-POWER update 2026-11-12.

**D157 (tactical, no design content).** The power slot is closed by a statement and a generated table drawn from the stored per-effect rows (power of a 4-point difference and the size of the test), not by a new simulation; no input of the simulation changed, and the table from graded runs stays an open slot.

**Citations.** The only open `\todo{cite}` was the human-validated subset of the benchmark. Its source is a web announcement (13 August 2024), not a paper; every fetch tool got HTTP 403 from the live page, and an Internet Archive capture of 31 December 2024 was read from title to footnotes (note `swe-bench-verified-announcement-2024.md`, SHA-256 of the extracted text recorded). The reading rule is defined on PDFs (`read: full — N pages ... PDF`), so the note carries the header `read: NOT READ (...)` with the reason and no numbers table, and **D156**: the report does not cite the announcement; it describes the subset through UTBoost (read in full, 13 pages), which restates the 500 instances and 93 annotators and cites the announcement, and it states that the release is not cited because the paper rule does not cover it. The test `test_citations_resolve_to_notes_read_in_full` was not changed. **Q75** (engineer): admit web sources under the reading rule with an archive capture, its hash and a named header form, so that the announcement can be cited for its construction (1,699 samples, three labels each, drop on any severe flag, the 500 drawn toward the hardest buckets)? Cheapest settling step: one line in `knowledge/papers/README.md` and a header form in `ril/util/paper_note.py`, about an hour; recommended: yes, with the capture hash, because the construction is not described anywhere else.

**Papers read in full (PDF from the paper's own arXiv page, `pdfinfo` equals extracted pages, no blank page; notes in `knowledge/papers/`).** Skip the Compressor (2610.21073, 24 pp, 31 numbers found), UTBoost (2506.09289, 13 pp, 31 numbers), contrastive confidence regularization (2401.00165, 14 pp, 20 numbers). Findings that bear on the project: a fixed-budget, test-graded comparison finds whole-file selection ahead of compressors and retrievers by 6.3 to 12.7 points, and a second patch sample flips 8.7 to 18.9% of outcomes (scale of run noise); the benchmark's log parser mislabels the stored test lists of 54.2% of the 500 and 26 of the 500 have tests that accept wrong patches (django and sympy dominate, as in the executable subset; the project's executor does not use that parser, and whether its test lists equal the corrected ones is not checked, now stated as an unchecked point in the note); a loss-level confidence regularizer assumes no false positives, so it does not address the noise model of the mined pairs. Discrepancies inside UTBoost (36 against 23 plus 26 instances; 345 against 234 and 171) are in its note. No contradiction with project text was found.

**Related work.** Nine papers that already had notes are now cited (SWE-Explore, GREPO, LARGER, Exploration Structure, MM-IssueLoc, Promptagator, GloFND, self-distillation with adaptive margins, fine-tuning failure), plus the three new ones; each claim has its note and page in a source comment; bibliography entries are from the notes' own headers (authors, title, venue, identifier). The dated search log is in `knowledge/papers/LANDSCAPE.md` ("Refresh of 2026-11-12": three queries, what each returned, leads still unread). The arXiv listing search was not rerun, so the window of 2026-10-23 is not extended. Not done: the second supervision-side paper of gap row 19 (Hidden Positives, listing only), leads named by the new papers (SWE-bench+, SWE-rebench, SWE-Pruner, SWEzze, the memorisation study), and Qwen3 Embedding, ARISE, D-SCL and the uncertainty-aware paper, which have notes but are not cited.

**Consistency proofreading (every fix recorded).** `scripts/check_report.py` guarantees a source comment on every prose number; it does not check that the comment names a real file or that the value equals the file. Added `missing_sources` (`ril/util/report_check.py`, in `check_report.py` and a test) and read the prose against the entries; spot values were compared with the stored files (377 executable of 500 and 228 for the largest project, 244 of 1,131 rejected prompts, F = 3,885 of 5,789, cell-table digest, power range, unit-level BM25 values +3.03, -0.28, +2.93) and equal them. Mismatches found: (1) **the model-choice rule** in the analysis plan was the cheapest-first rule of D112, superseded by D155 (budget and floor, highest resolved count, the cheaper within two instances): text and source comment rewritten; (2) three source comments named a tables file `unit_bm25_variants_dev` that does not exist (the values are in `unit_bm25_compare.csv`): comments corrected; (3) gap-map row 24 said the launch entry was not on main: the launch status (design and state only: 45 configurations, one digest, 41 of 41 tests, six jobs, F = 3,885, N = 100, the dense-teacher cell on and run later under its own authorisation) is now in the report, with no result; (4) gap-map rows 5 and 19 and the state file's slot count (23 against 19 found by the checker before this pass) are updated. No RQ2 or RQ3 result, resolved rate or audited noise rate appears in the report (searched: the only resolved-rate figures are cited papers' and the 15% floor).

**Result of the pass.** `check_report.py`: 0 findings, 16 open slots (19 before; the cite, the refresh and the power slot are closed); the report compiles with tectonic (20 pages, 0 overfull boxes).

**Dead ends.** (a) The OpenAI page could not be fetched live; the archive route worked. (b) The first draft cited the announcement and failed the project's own citation test, which is the reason for D156 rather than a test change. (c) A web search for an "executable subset of the verified benchmark" found no defining paper; the project's executable subset (D106) stays its own definition.

**Not known.** Whether the project's executor's test lists equal UTBoost's corrected lists (cheapest check: compare the 377 instances' named tests with the corrected annotations, CPU, about a day of work; not done); whether the announcement's archived capture equals the 2024-08 original (the capture is 4.5 months later; the page text carries the 13 August date).

## E-RQ2-DEV-ANALYSIS: completion check of the RQ2 final training runs and the development-set analysis, 2026-11-13 (decision D160)

Status: **done.** All 42 planned runs finished and passed the completion check; the analysis ran once on the development set with the stored rules, and the dev-analysis record that unseals the final sets was written by the module's own writer. Evidence class: measured (stored per-run results, 5 development repositories at L2 and 2 at L3, 3 seeds per cell, 100 updates per run). Nothing of the eight repositories or the executable 377 was read.

**Readings taken.** (a) The planned scope is 42 runs: 15 mined on account C and 27 injected on account A. The 45 registered runs include the three FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small mined runs; that cell is outside this launch (D153) and is not in this check or this analysis. (b) "The leakage check of the run": the leakage and torch tests of the launch commit passed on both accounts (41 of 41, `results/rq2_launch/leakage_ok_A.json`, `leakage_ok_C.json`), and every run's training record states that the quality-signal encoder is not the encoder being trained. (c) "The checkpoint exists": for the 15 mined runs, the retained model folder and its manifest name the registered configuration hash; the injected runs retain no model by design (D142), so for them the finished training record and the stored results are the evidence. (d) Resubmissions and node faults are decision D160 below.

**Resubmissions (D160, tactical).** Two kinds of node fault, both transient and none a code, data or leakage defect. Account C: the seed 101 and 102 jobs (73579, 73580) started on a node whose torch cannot start CUDA and stopped at the CUDA gate in under 3 minutes each, no training; resubmitted as 73833 and 73834 with identical runs, seed, configuration, commit and result date, and the known-bad nodes excluded. Account A: the seed 101 job (73582) ran 20 minutes on a node that reports the `2080ti` feature but holds an RTX 3080 with 10 GiB and stopped with a CUDA out-of-memory in a backward pass; the launch plan documents no fix for that failure, so this is a placement reading, not a documented rule (stated for the lead to confirm); resubmitted as 74023, which landed on a node with the same broken CUDA device (the exclusion list I passed was incomplete) and stopped at the gate in 52 seconds, then as 74358 with the full list; completed. The configuration, seeds and update counts of every run are unchanged; the only difference to the launch submit lines is the node exclusion list. Runs that had finished inside a failed job are skipped by the resubmitted job. Ledger rows carry the account letter only.

**Completion check (`ril/bench/rq2_completion.py`, `scripts/rq2_completion_check.py`, 3 unit tests).** 42 of 42 runs: result directory with the registered configuration hash, stored hash equal, 100 optimizer updates, training marked finished, seed equal, leakage record passed; 15 of 15 retained models present with matching manifest hashes (C). Per-run table in `results/logs/rq2_final/<run id>/` (`results.json`, `config.yaml`, `hardware.json`) and `results/rq2_final/dev/dev_runs.csv`. Run counts by cell: mined RAW, FILTER-BM25, subsample, WEIGHT, CURRICULUM 3 each (C); injected RAW, FILTER-BM25, ORACLE-CLEAN, subsample, clean RAW, FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small, WEIGHT, RAW at 0.1, FILTER-BM25 at 0.1, 3 each (A); seeds 100, 101, 102. Training-loop GPU-hours summed from the run records: 1.25 (C) and 2.01 (A). **GPU-hours by job (`sacct` elapsed, one GPU): account C 6.16, account A 9.99, 16.15 in all, of which 0.43 is failed or aborted jobs (A 0.35, C 0.08); plus 1.58 for the launch stage (smoke, retention check, teacher measurement): 17.73 of the Block 3 cap of 46.7.** The sealed scoring is still to come against the 15.44 projected for it.

**Dev analysis.** `scripts/rq2_rows.py dev` on the 42 result directories: 21,252 rows (42 runs x 506 queries: 351 L2, 155 L3); `scripts/rq2_analysis.py` with the module defaults (seed 0, 2,000 draws, alpha 0.05, delta 1.5), run once; summary `results/rq2_final/dev/dev_analysis_summary.json` (analysis hash `77ab8fafc7e8...`, input hashes inside; rows SHA-256 `91846141...cf49`, runs `7b48216b...d048`), 48 contrasts, contrast table `results/rq2_final/dev/dev_contrasts.csv`. Intervals as stored: the decision interval is the cluster-robust t interval over repositories with R - 1 degrees of freedom (D136), adjusted by Holm within the family; the percentile bootstrap over whole repositories is beside it; at L3 the dev set has 2 repositories, so every query is its own cluster (query-level interval, 154 degrees of freedom) and the repository bootstrap is not defined. Holm adjustment is the family of the contrast on one evaluation set, as coded.

Confirmatory family (FILTER-BM25 against RAW and against the size-matched subsample on the mined set; primary metric of each level), `mined`, values in points, as stored:

| Contrast | Level, metric | Estimate | t interval (uncorrected) | Bootstrap | Holm-adjusted t interval | Adjusted p | Rule outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FILTER-BM25 - RAW | L2 nDCG@10 (5 repositories) | +0.24 | [-1.16, +1.64] | [-0.62, +1.00] | [-1.94, +2.41] | 1.00 | not rejected; inconclusive |
| FILTER-BM25 - subsample | L2 nDCG@10 | -0.37 | [-1.48, +0.75] | [-1.12, +0.33] | [-2.10, +1.37] | 1.00 | not rejected; estimate with bounds |
| FILTER-BM25 - RAW | L3 Recall@100 (2 repositories, query-level) | -0.21 | [-0.98, +0.56] | not defined | [-1.19, +0.77] | 1.00 | not rejected; estimate with bounds |
| FILTER-BM25 - subsample | L3 Recall@100 | -0.34 | [-1.03, +0.34] | not defined | [-1.22, +0.54] | 1.00 | not rejected; estimate with bounds |

All four are indistinguishable within the pooled seed SD of the two cells (seed SD of the difference 0.62, 0.39, 1.03, 0.73), and the per-seed differences change sign across the three seeds in all four. **None of the confirmatory contrasts is distinguishable from no effect at this sample size; the arms-separate rule of D80 (FILTER-BM25 against RAW positive at both levels on a final set) is not met on the development set.** Cell means (L2 nDCG@10 / L3 Recall@100, three seeds): mined RAW 26.74 / 47.37, FILTER-BM25 26.97 / 47.16, subsample 27.34 / 47.50, WEIGHT 26.90 / 47.37, CURRICULUM 26.38 / 46.85.

Exploratory contrasts (mined WEIGHT and CURRICULUM against RAW and the subsample; the injected family against RAW and the subsample): of the 24 contrasts that carry a Holm-adjusted decision, 6 are rejected, all of them injected-family contrasts at L3 Recall@100 (ORACLE-CLEAN, FILTER-BM25, FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small and WEIGHT against RAW; FILTER-BM25 and FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small against the subsample; estimates +1.10 to +2.85 points, query-level intervals, two repositories). No mined contrast is rejected. At L2 nDCG@10 no injected contrast is rejected after adjustment. Injected-noise sanity as stored: RAW at r = 0.4 is below clean RAW (L2 -2.79, interval excludes 0; L3 -3.14, interval excludes 0); the oracle recovers at L3 (+2.85, interval excludes 0), not at L2 (+1.85, interval includes 0); the L2 reading is not informative by the stored rule, the L3 reading is. Dose-response, FILTER-BM25 at r = 0.1 against 0.4: gain difference +0.87 at L2 nDCG@10, interval includes 0, "rises with r" false as stored. These are the stored rules' outputs; no rule was changed after seeing a number. **Hardware found at the check (not a rule change; for the lead).** The jobs asked for a 2080 Ti and 36 of the 42 runs trained on one (`hardware.json` of each run). Six did not: all five mined arms of seed 101 trained on an RTX 4070 (job 73833, one node), and injected RAW of seed 101 trained on an RTX 3080 (job 73582, the node of the out-of-memory, before it failed; the resubmitted job skipped the finished run). Within a seed, the arms of the mined family share one GPU model, so the paired contrasts are unaffected by it; for the injected family RAW of seed 101 sits on another GPU model than the arms it is compared with (RAW is the reference of every injected contrast). The effect of the GPU model on a run was not measured here (fp16 training, same seed and configuration; the retention check compared one model with itself). Cheapest check: re-run mined seed 101 and injected RAW seed 101 on a 2080 Ti (6 runs, about 2.5 GPU-hours of the 12.36 margin, one job per account) and compare per-query rows; or a labelled exploratory read of the dev analysis without seed 101. Neither was run: a re-run is a new run outside the registered 42 and needs the lead's authorization, and the stored analysis was applied as written. The contrast tables above carry this caveat. Questions for the lead: (1) confirm the placement reading of the A out-of-memory; (2) whether to re-run the six runs above on a 2080 Ti; (3) the eight questions already listed in E-RQ2-ANALYSIS stand.

**Dev-analysis record.** `results/rq2_final/dev_analysis_record.json`, written by `final_sets.write_dev_analysis_record` with this entry's id, the 42 run ids and a digest of the 42 stored `results.json` files (`results/logs/rq2_final/`). The final-sets scoring follows this entry (D100).

**Not done.** The sealed scoring of the 15 retained mined models on the eight repositories and the executable 377, and everything after it; the FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small mined cell (needs its own authorization, D153). **Dead ends.** Three failed jobs from node faults (above); a first monitoring pattern that matched text saying an entry was absent and opened a gate early (caught at once, nothing was started).
