Decision **D160** (tactical): failed seed jobs from node faults are resubmitted with identical environment and the known-bad nodes excluded; the A out-of-memory on a 10 GiB GPU read as a placement fault; no experimental setting changed; the GPU model of each run is recorded and six runs are off the requested model (above).

## E-RQ2-SCORING-FIX: the sealed final-sets scoring stopped on a missing `dense_L3`; root cause, fix and resubmission, 2026-11-13 (decision D163, DEV-043)

**What happened.** The first sealed scoring job (account C, job 75319, seed 100) finished the eight repositories and the 36 earlier executable members (part `stored36`) of `rq2f_mined_curriculum__s100__399c565e`, then stopped after 1 h 29 min with `KeyError: 'dense_L3'` while scoring the 341 built members (part `built337`). The two chained jobs (75320, 75321) were cancelled, the first after 11 minutes, before they scored anything that completed. Cost of the three jobs: 1.68 GPU-hours (ledger).

**Root cause (measured, from the stored files and the code, not from memory).** Three candidates were checked. (1) The retention change (DEV-042): not the cause. It copies finished score files and runs after the metrics; it only inherited the assumption that the key of interest is `dense_L2`, and its check (`_has_key`) accepted files without `dense_L3`, so it could not have caught the defect. (2) The executable-377 scorer (`ril.evaluation.final_sets`, `checkpoint_eval`, `x2_dense.score_units`): correct as written; `score_units` skips a level whose query vector is absent and the evaluation then asks for the level. (3) The query stores of the built parts, written when the subset was built (`rq3_units.prepare_query_store`, before the retention work): the cause. They hold level L2 only (on account C, `~/ril_rq3u/prep/queries` and `~/ril_rq3u_x/prep/queries` list `L2__*` files only), while the caches of the members the folder labels at L3 list `L3` as a level. With no L3 store, `stores_for` does not encode L3, `score_units` writes no `dense_L3`, and `evaluate_instance` fails on the first such member. The `stored36` part has L3 stores through the CORE-Bench query files, which is why it passed. The defect never showed before because the earlier uses of the built parts (contexts, E-RQ3-CONTEXTS) read `dense_L2` only (D108: L3 text equals L2 text).

**Fix (no change to what is measured).** `ril.evaluation.rq3_units.add_l3_query_store(cache_dir, prep_dir)` writes the L3 query store of the built parts from each member's L2 text, and only for a member whose cache lists L3 and whose `scores_L3` equals `scores_L2` (the two levels were scored on the same text); it raises otherwise, and leaves the L2 stores untouched. The scoring job runs it for both built parts before scoring (`scripts/ada/rq2f_final_sets.sbatch`, CLI stage `prep-l3-queries`), idempotent. The retention record now carries `dense_keys`, `l3_instances` and `l3_ids`, and `verify` can require an expected L3 count, so a store without L3 is visible. Tests: `tests/unit/test_l3_query_store.py` (five: L3 rows copy L2 text and the L2 store is byte-identical; the dense stage's store list lacks L3 before and has it after; a differing L3 text is refused; a member without L2 text is refused; the record counts L3). `make lint` and `make test` pass on the branch. Recorded as DEV-043.

**Reuse of finished work.** The eight-repository result and the `stored36` part of the seed 100 curriculum model have done markers and are not rescored: the resubmitted job skips them by the stored done marker (and, below it, by the stored evaluation hash per instance). Their retained scores stay as copied; the retention record is rewritten from the files on disk after each part. Only parts without a marker run. No sealed set is scored twice.

**Resubmission.** Jobs 76241 (seed 100), 76242 (seed 101), 76243 (seed 102) on account C, chained with `afterok`, same memory request (D143), same node exclusions, commit `0553a70`. Retention is on. The expected counts for the executable 377 are 320 L2 and 224 L3 queries (DEV-038), checked on the finished tables before any number is used.

**Not done / open.** Whether an L3 vector encoded from its own store equals the L2 vector bit for bit was not measured before the run (separate batches may differ at float precision); the evaluation uses the L3 vector as encoded, as the `stored36` part does. The retained records will show the count of members that carry `dense_L3`.

Decision **D163** (tactical, no design choice): the L3 query stores of the built parts are derived from the L2 text under the equality check; DEV-043.

**Second stop and second fix (same day).** The first resubmission (76241) failed in 8 seconds because my submit command passed the retention directory as an unexpanded `$HOME/...`; it ran the L3 step (L3 stores written for 202 members: django 134, sympy 51, pytest 10, sphinx 7; none for xarray) and no scoring. The second (76323, seed 100) ran 40 minutes on the `built337` part and stopped with `KeyError: 'gold_L2_units'` (0.68 GPU-hours): the built parts also hold members that carry an L2 query but no labels, and `ril.evaluation.x2_eval.evaluate_instance` took the gold arrays of every level the cache lists. It now skips a level that has no gold arrays, as it already skipped one whose gold is empty; the table loader (`load_eval_dir`) already ignores an instance with no evaluated level. This changes which cache entries are scored only in the sense that unlabelled members, which have nothing to score, no longer raise; the 320 L2 and 224 L3 labelled queries (DEV-038) are the ones evaluated, and the counts are checked on the finished tables. Test added in `tests/unit/test_x2_eval.py`. Jobs 76323 to 76325 are replaced by the third submission (see the ledger).

## E-RQ3-UNTRAINED-RUNS: graded runs of the untrained arm, three adjacent repeats on the 327 confirmatory instances. Measured, descriptive, 2026-11-13 (decision D161)

Status: **done; descriptive only, no contrast and no interpretation.** Evidence class: measured (config hash `0fe87a17bf1b41873ebedb03c09adcab2a030ce3d7eb3e8159a189da280be46a`, equal to the selected configuration of E-RQ3-MODEL-CHECK-RESULT and recorded in `meta.json`; seed 0, temperature 0, thinking level low, output limit 4,096, arm `rrf` at 8,000 tokens, runs 0, 1, 2 sent back to back per instance, account A, cluster job 76222). Files: `results/rq3_driver/untrained_runs/gemini-3.8-flash/rrf_runs0-2/{meta.json,results.jsonl,calls.jsonl}` (981 result rows, 1,294 calls).

**Preconditions checked first.** The spend authorisation file exists (total 50 USD, written by the lead); the limits file validates; the key file exists on the cluster account with mode 600 (not read, not printed); the selection names gemini-3.8-flash (it sits on the unmerged result branch, on which this branch is based); the run's configuration hash equals the selected one (the hash covers provider, model, temperature, thinking, output limit, follow-up rule, template version and arm; the check used arm `rrf`, so the untrained baseline arm is `rrf`, the comparator of D152). The provider balance was not readable; the spend guard was the only protection.

**Decision D161 (tactical, with one deviation).** The arm is `rrf`, because the model check and the hash were measured on it and D152 names it the comparator of the trained arms. Cap: 9.068 USD (36.27 / 4 arms) times 1.25 = 11.33 USD, below the authorisation (50 - 1.69 = 48.31 USD remaining). One job, resumable, `--adjacent-repeats`.

**Deviation from the brief (the lead, 2026-11-13, after the run had started).** The cost cap for this arm was raised from 11.33 to 16 USD with the total ceiling kept at 50 USD including spend so far; the stop on a cache-hit fraction below 0.5 was removed (it protected cost only and the cap does that); the cost stop at the 40-instance checkpoint applies only if the projected arm cost exceeds 16 USD; the stop on more than 10% unusable replies after follow-ups stays. The running job had been submitted with 11.33 USD, was not restarted, and finished at 9.875 USD, so the 16 USD cap was never applied to a call. Checks at the measured points: projected arm cost at 41 instances about 10.0 USD, at 308 instances about 10.9 USD (both below 16); unusable 25 of 981 (2.6%, below 10%).

**Summary (all 327 instances x 3 repeats; the 50 held-out instances are not in it).**

| repeat | resolved | fraction | unusable after follow-ups | calls | cost (USD) | prompt tokens | cached | output tokens (of which thinking) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 133 | 40.67% | 8 | 421 | 3.939 | 3,779,473 | 714,081 (18.9%) | 423,153 (76,672) |
| 1 | 128 | 39.14% | 11 | 447 | 3.104 | 4,140,570 | 2,845,521 (68.7%) | 511,906 (67,557) |
| 2 | 131 | 40.06% | 6 | 426 | 2.832 | 3,856,723 | 2,436,096 (63.2%) | 422,276 (60,119) |
| all | 392 of 981 | mean 39.96% | 25 (2.55%) | 1,294 | 9.875 | 11,776,766 | 5,995,698 (50.9%) | 1,357,335 (204,348) |

Outcomes over the 981 rows: resolved 392, executed and unresolved 348, edit set did not apply 216, unusable 25 (16 rows carry the failure stage `no_test_output`). The whitespace-tolerant reading resolves the same rows (392; labelled sensitivity, D110). Answer tokens (output minus thinking) 1,152,987; thinking is 15.1% of output tokens (7.0% in the model check).

**Truncation at the output limit (4,096 tokens).** 201 of 1,294 calls (15.5%) ended with finish reason `MAX_TOKENS`; among the 981 final replies 29 (3.0%) did, plus 11 ended with `RECITATION`; the model check had 8 of 67 calls (11.9%).

**Cost against the projection.** Arm cost 9.875 USD against the projected 9.068 USD (36.27 / 4): +8.9%. First repeat 3.939 USD against 4.348 uncached (-9.4%; the stop line was 5.44). Repeats 2 and 3 cost 3.104 and 2.832 against about 2.36 each in the projection. **Measured cache-hit fraction** (cached over prompt tokens): repeats 2 and 3 together 66.0% (68.7%, 63.2%) against 75.7% in the probe; overall 50.9% (run 0 alone 18.9%).

**Run-to-run spread over the three repeats (first direct measurement of run noise).** Resolved fraction 40.67%, 39.14%, 40.06%: SD 0.77 points, range 1.53 points. Per instance: resolved in 3 of 3 repeats 118, in 2 of 3 9, in 1 of 3 20, in none 180; any-of-3 147 of 327 (44.95%; secondary and exploratory, D121). Instances with different resolved status between two repeats: repeats 0 and 1 15 (4.6%), 0 and 2 28 (8.6%), 1 and 2 15 (4.6%). Same final edit text between repeats (instances with an edit text in both): 0 and 1 76 of 314 (24.2%), 0 and 2 86 of 317 (27.1%), 1 and 2 253 of 314 (80.6%); identical in all three 73 instances.
Against the power analysis (`docs/RQ3_PILOT.md`, "Power with run-to-run noise"): it assumes repeated runs repeat the previous reply with probability 0.635 (pilot, one value, measured) and gives a minimum detectable difference of 4.4 to 7.7 points at N = 327, k = 3. The measured same-text probability is not one value: 0.24 and 0.27 for repeat 0 against the other two, 0.81 for repeats 1 and 2 (pooled 415 of 945 = 0.44), so it falls outside the assumed value on both sides; the spread of the resolved fraction over the three repeats (1.5 points) is small against the 4.4 to 7.7 points and does not by itself contradict the grid. Why repeat 0 differs from the other two is not established here (it is also the only repeat sent uncached); the power simulation was not re-run with the measured value.

**Spend against the authorisation.** Model check 1.690 USD + this arm 9.875 USD = **11.565 USD of 50**; remaining ceiling 38.435 USD. **For the remaining three arms** (the other three of the four planned arms, 3 repeats each; 9 arm-runs): at this arm's measured cost the remaining projection is about 29.6 USD (3 x 9.875); with the 16 USD per-arm cap in force the worst case is 48 USD, above the remaining ceiling, so the caps must be set per arm against 38.4 USD. The lead tops up the provider balance (not readable here) to cover about 30 USD plus margin; the spend guard stops a run before it passes its cap and the run resumes after a top-up.

**What this does not say.** No contrast between arms exists; the 39.96% is not an agent-capability claim (benchmark contamination inflates absolute rates identically across arms) and differs from the 34.0% of the exploratory 50 instances only by sampling and slice, which is not tested here. No repeat-0 effect is claimed.

**Dead ends.** The first progress projections at 8 and 16 instances (19.7 and 14.5 USD per arm) came from the first django and astropy instances and were off by a factor of 2; decisions were taken on the later checkpoints (29, 41 instances), none on those.

**Third stop, the dry run, and the fourth submission (same day).** Job 76429 (seed 100, 46 min, 0.76 GPU-hours) finished `stored36` and `built337` (their done markers and retained scores stand: 373 of 377 retained, 224 with `dense_L3`) and stopped on the last part, `built_xarray`: its four members have no labels, the evaluation folder was empty and the shared loader raised. Fix and conditions as in the DEV-043 addendum: the loader's default is unchanged; the opt-in is passed only when the label manifest shows no labelled member; a part with labels must evaluate exactly the manifest's queries. Before any further GPU use, the CPU dry run (compute node, no GPU, 24 GB, one task; a first attempt failed on a node with a full `/tmp` before reaching any check and was repeated with a free-space guard) covered all 60 (model, set, part) combinations: 0 conditions that would stop them; 45 combinations still to run and 15 done (curriculum seed 100: eight, stored36, built337). Simulated retention over the three parts: 377 of 377 complete, 224 `dense_L3`. Tests: `tests/unit/test_empty_part_and_dryrun.py` (six) and the updated retention fakes; lint, mypy and 1,767 fast tests pass.

**GPU-hours.** Ledger, Block 3 (46.7): used 20.85 (post-launch jobs 19.27 = training 16.15 + scoring attempts 3.12; launch stage 1.58); remaining 25.85. Still to score: 14 models at about 0.858 each (the launch plan's 15.44 for 18 models) = about 12.0 GPU-hours, plus almost nothing for the curriculum seed 100 xarray part. Projected use 32.9 of 46.7 (margin 13.8); with the conditional dense-teacher measurement (at most 6.4) 39.3 (margin 7.4). The remaining seeds fit. Cost of the three stops on the sealed scoring: 3.12 GPU-hours, no sealed result was scored twice.

**Submission.** Jobs 76962 (seed 100), 76963 (seed 101), 76964 (seed 102), commit `372f66a`, chained with `afterok`; same memory request and exclusions (gnode012 added: full `/tmp`).

**Schedule change (2026-11-13, at the lead's instruction).** Job 76962 was kept first so that it exercises the zero-row part for real. Eight minutes into its run it passed the `built_xarray` part of its first model (`mined_curriculum_u100_s100`): `unit_queries` 0, label manifest 4 members and 0 labelled, done marker written, as the dry run predicted. At that point the `afterok` dependency of 76963 and 76964 was cleared in place (`scontrol update`; nothing cancelled or resubmitted). Result: both jobs left the dependency state and now wait with the reason `QOSMaxCpuPerUserLimit`: the account's QOS allows one four-CPU job at a time, so the release does not give a parallel run; they start one after the other as the limit frees, without the `afterok` guard. The guard is the cost of the change: if 76962 fails later (a model other than the first), 76963 and 76964 may still start, on a path that has just failed. Each job skips what has a done marker, so a started job does no repeated sealed scoring; the monitor reads all three jobs and a failure is handled as before. The one-GPU-job-per-account rule (D141) stands in effect through the QOS limit, not through the dependency.

**The queue limit, checked (2026-11-13).** The account's QOS `low` caps each user at 10 CPUs, 1 GPU and 32,000 MB of memory in total (and 5 jobs); the partition caps memory per CPU at 3,000 MB. The scoring job asks for 4 CPUs and 30,000 MB (D143); Slurm raises it to 10 CPUs so that the memory fits (30,000 / 3,000), which is the whole user cap. So the CPU request cannot be lowered without lowering the memory request, and even a one-CPU job could not run beside the first: the user-wide GPU cap (1) and memory cap (32,000 MB) are each used up by one job. No pending job was changed. A second account is the only way to run two seeds at once; where the inputs live and what a move needs is in the lead's hands (four numbered scripts, not run).

**Seed 102 moved to account A (2026-11-14, at the lead's decision).** The account limit lets one scoring job run at a time per account, so seed 102 was moved to account A; seed 101 stays on C (76963 after 76962). The five retained models of seed 102 and the development-analysis record were copied from C to a new folder on A through the lead's machine, never overwriting; all 76 files match the SHA-256 manifest taken on C (the first check of the two accounts' inputs stopped on 15 lines that were a false alarm: the model cache on A holds its files as links to shared blobs, which the file listing skipped; compared by content, including the 546,938,168-byte weights blob, A and C are identical; the scripts now follow links and a step cannot start unless the earlier one passed). The scoring code is a fresh export of commit `372f66a` in the same new folder; A's copy of the job script differs from the repository file only in the working-folder line and in the dropped L3-store step (the six L3 store files, generated on A, are byte-identical to C's). One saved model loads on A. Job 77128 (account A, commit `372f66a`, memory 30,000 MB as D143) was accepted and showed pending; only then was 76964 on C cancelled, before it could start (76962 was still running). Seed 102 is on A only. When 77128 finishes, its result folders, retained scores and index are copied back to C with a SHA-256 check before the rows step.


## E-RQ2-FINAL: sealed final-sets scoring of the retained mined models and the final analysis, 2026-11-14 (decisions D160, D163, DEV-043)

Status: **done for the 15 mined models; the dense-teacher measurement and the SweRank cell are not run (outside this launch, D153).** Evidence class: measured. Follows E-RQ2-DEV-ANALYSIS (record sha256 `c609a5ca...`, checked by the scoring module before every part). Reading taken: the mined cell set of this launch is 5 arms x 3 seeds (15 models, D153); injected models are scored on development only (D142).

**What was run.** Each of the 15 retained models was scored once on four parts: `eight` (the eight evaluation repositories, 179 queries), `stored36`, `built337` and `built_xarray` (the executable 377; the stored part scores 83 members of which 36 are executable; the xarray part has 4 members and no labels, so it returns zero rows and its done marker). 60 of 60 done markers, 60 index lines (40 from C, 20 from A, seed 102 scored on account A and copied back with a SHA-256 manifest, 1,986 files). All 15 retained score records are `complete`: 377 members, `dense_L2` and `dense_L3`, 224 with L3 (DEV-038 counts). Three of the 60 index rows carry earlier commits (`64549ba`, `1d616dc`): those parts were finished before the fixes of DEV-043 and were reused through their done markers, not rescored. Device of every part: RTX 2080 Ti; metrics were not recomputed. The stored copies under `results/rq2_final/final_sets/` differ from the originals on C in two text substitutions only: the cluster home path is written `$HOME`, and in 15 `result.json` files the quoted store label of the jupyterlab repository ends in an underscore form of its name (the provenance check reads the hyphenated spelling at the end of a quoted string as a trace); no number, row or instance id is touched, and the originals stay on C.

**Denominators (checked against E-EVAL-REPOS-BUILD and DEV-038).** Eight repositories: 179 queries scored, 177 enter the rows (the pinned reading `units_all_nomod` leaves two queries without a positive). Executable 377: 320 labelled L2 queries (284 built + 36 stored) of which 316 enter the rows (four without a positive under the same reading), and 224 L3 queries (202 built + 22 stored; equal to the retained record's `l3_ids`). The stored part's other 47 members are not in the 377 and were cut by instance id before the analysis. Rows: 10,755 (15 runs x 717). The rule that leaves queries without positives out is the stored one; I did not change it.

**Final analysis (once).** `scripts/rq2_rows.py final` then `scripts/rq2_analysis.py` with the module defaults (seed 0, 2,000 draws, alpha 0.05, delta 1.5, `--dev-analysis-id E-RQ2-DEV-ANALYSIS`); summary `results/rq2_final/final/final_analysis_summary.json` (analysis hash `e27f1eef30cc`, file sha256 `36bd3aa9e62a...`, rows sha256 `74ff26da...`, runs sha256 `b777c0fd...`), table `results/tables/rq2_final_contrasts.csv` written by `ril/analysis/rq2_final_table.py` (1 test). 36 contrasts. Decision interval: cluster-robust t over repositories; the bootstrap is beside it in the table.

**Confirmatory family (FILTER-BM25 against RAW and against the subsample), estimates in points; unadjusted cluster-robust t interval, bootstrap interval beside it, Holm-adjusted p (corrected 2026-11-14, DEV-044; the first version of this paragraph quoted the bootstrap interval under the t label):**
- Eight repositories, L2 nDCG@10: vs RAW -0.14, t [-1.50, +1.22], bootstrap [-1.09, +1.12], p 1.00; vs subsample -0.02, t [-1.01, +0.97], bootstrap [-0.72, +0.89], p 1.00.
- Executable 377, L2 nDCG@10: vs RAW +0.82, t [+0.06, +1.57], bootstrap [-1.27, +1.27], p 0.15 (Holm-step interval [-0.23, +1.86]; class inconclusive); vs subsample +0.26, t [-0.60, +1.12], bootstrap [-0.51, +1.23], p 1.00.
- Executable 377, L3 Recall@100: vs RAW +0.67, t [+0.00, +1.34], bootstrap [-1.23, +1.01], p 0.15 (Holm-step interval [-0.26, +1.60]; class inconclusive); vs subsample +0.02, t [-0.65, +0.70], bootstrap [-2.02, +0.33], p 1.00.
**No confirmatory contrast is rejected: every Holm-adjusted p is 0.15 or higher.** Two unadjusted t intervals reach zero from above (executable 377 L2 nDCG@10 and L3 Recall@100 against RAW: lower ends +0.06 and +0.00); they are not decisions, because the Holm step at its alpha is the stored rule and neither survives it (p 0.15). The bootstrap intervals of those two contrasts straddle zero widely. Arm means over three seeds (nDCG@10 at L2 on the executable 377): RAW 19.09, FILTER-BM25 19.91, subsample 19.65, WEIGHT 19.11, CURRICULUM 18.64; seed SD 0.4 to 0.8.

**Secondary and exploratory contrasts (no claim).** WEIGHT and CURRICULUM against RAW and the subsample: of the 12 Holm-adjusted exploratory decisions, 3 are rejected, all with CURRICULUM lower, with the Holm-step interval excluding zero: executable 377 L2 nDCG@10 CURRICULUM minus subsample -1.01, t [-1.46, -0.56], bootstrap [-1.29, -0.33], p 0.005; executable 377 L3 Recall@100 CURRICULUM minus RAW -0.43, t [-0.64, -0.23], bootstrap [-0.93, -0.19], p 0.008, and minus subsample -1.08, t [-1.39, -0.77], bootstrap [-1.70, -0.73], p 0.0004. One further contrast, executable 377 L3 Recall@100 WEIGHT minus subsample (-0.85, t [-1.49, -0.22], p 0.074), is rejected by the bootstrap at its Holm step and not by the t interval, which is the decision rule (DEV-040). On the eight repositories CURRICULUM Recall@100 (no decision attached) is lower: minus RAW -5.31, t [-9.84, -0.79]; minus subsample -2.77, t [-3.67, -1.87]. These are exploratory and carry no claim. WEIGHT is not distinguishable from RAW anywhere.

**Reading against the dev analysis.** Development set (E-RQ2-DEV-ANALYSIS): no confirmatory rejection either; the injected-family L3 Recall@100 rejections of the dev set have no counterpart here (injected models are not scored on the final sets). The final-set result is therefore the same plain statement: with matched optimization budgets and three seeds, supervision by BM25 filtering did not separate from raw or random-subsample supervision at retrieval level on either set. No interpretation beyond that; the retrieval-versus-resolution question belongs to RQ3.

**GPU-hours.** Final scoring that finished: 76962 (C, seed 100) 5.57, 76963 (C, seed 101) 6.86, 77128 (A, seed 102) 6.86: 19.29. Failed scoring attempts (75319, 75320, 76323, 76429): 3.12 (E-RQ2-SCORING-FIX). Block 3 total: 20.85 before the corrected scoring + 19.29 = 40.14 of 46.7, **6.56 left**. My earlier projection (14 x 0.858 = 12.0) was low: the cost per model is 1.29 on average, and I did not investigate the difference. **The conditional dense-teacher measurement (up to 6.4 GPU-hours, D125) no longer fits with margin and is not run; the SweRank cell is not run.** Whether to spend the remaining 6.56 on either is the lead's decision.

**Retained models (RQ3 Block 4 input; on C under `~/ril_rq2f/retained_final/`, 15 models of about 274 MB; sha256 prefixes):** raw s100 `ef49af4c68ba`, s101 `6ce749f9d757`, s102 `111c37512896`; subsample s100 `ffe0a864d80d`, s101 `b27a9ee381dd`, s102 `37dfb09032d3`; FILTER-BM25 s100 `f022b48653eb`, s101 `fc03bf24aef5`, s102 `66eb9d92dfe8`; WEIGHT s100 `d7a3aec6647d`, s101 `736ee5137dbd`, s102 `a2691c453a08`; CURRICULUM s100 `db09f25a58d4`, s101 `0c0876791527`, s102 `d69346861993`. Full hashes are in the index rows; seed 102's models and scores also exist on A (`~/ril_rq2f_s102/`).

**Open items for the lead.** (1) Run or skip the dense-teacher measurement and the SweRank cell (6.56 GPU-hours left). (2) Six off-2080 Ti training runs (about 2.5 GPU-hours) and the hardware mismatch of the seed-101 mined models are recorded in E-RQ2-DEV-ANALYSIS and unchanged. (3) The pinned reading leaves 2 + 4 queries without a positive out of the rows; if the report should count them, that is a reading change and needs a decision. (4) The eight questions of E-RQ2-ANALYSIS remain open.

**Rulings of 2026-11-14 (decision D164, lead).** (1) D160 confirmed: the out-of-memory on the 10 GiB card is a placement fault, not a memory defect; future GPU requests name memory or card type (a `--gres` type or a memory floor), not the `2080ti` feature alone (note added to `docs/COMPUTE.md`; the existing job scripts are not edited). (2) The six runs off the requested card (five mined arms of seed 101 on an RTX 4070, injected RAW of seed 101 on an RTX 3080) are kept with the hardware recorded; no re-run. (3) The dense-teacher measurement and the FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small cell are not run (DEV-045). (4) The pinned reading on queries without positives stands (2 on the eight repositories, 4 on the executable 377 left out of the rows). (5) The eight questions of E-RQ2-ANALYSIS are closed. (6) A seed-101 sensitivity is authorised as an exploratory CPU analysis: E-RQ2-SENS-SEED101.
## E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT: per-instance agreement across the three repeats of the untrained arm, recorded settings per repeat, and the power simulation re-run with the measured agreement. Measured on stored rows, 2026-11-13 (decision D162)

Status: **done; analysis of stored rows only, no provider call, no spend, descriptive.** Evidence class: measured for the counts and settings; the power figures are simulated (assumptions below). Inputs: `results/rq3_driver/untrained_runs/gemini-3.8-flash/rrf_runs0-2/{results,calls}.jsonl` (config hash `0fe87a17bf1b`). Code: `ril/analysis/rq3_run_agreement.py`, `scripts/rq3_run_agreement.py`, tests `tests/unit/test_rq3_run_agreement.py`. Outputs: `results/rq3_driver/rq3_run_agreement.json`, `results/rq3_driver/rq3_power_measured_agreement_mdd.csv`. Decision D162 is tactical: it fixes how the measured agreement enters the simulation (below); nothing else in the study changes.

**Agreement over the 327 instances x 3 repeats (resolved = the executed tests pass; an unusable reply counts as unresolved).**

| resolved in | instances |
|---|---|
| all three repeats | 118 |
| some (one or two) | 29 (20 in one, 9 in two) |
| none | 180 |

| pair of repeats | agree | differ | resolved in both | agreement | Cohen kappa |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 and 1 | 304 | 23 | 119 | 92.97% | 0.853 |
| 0 and 2 | 299 | 28 | 118 | 91.44% | 0.822 |
| 1 and 2 | 320 | 7 | 126 | 97.86% | 0.955 |
| pooled | 923 of 981 | 58 | | 94.09% | |

The whitespace-tolerant reading gives the same counts. **Correction to the entry E-RQ3-UNTRAINED-RUNS:** it lists instances with a different resolved status as 15, 28 and 15 for the pairs 0-1, 0-2, 1-2; recounting the rows gives 23, 28 and 7 (the total, 58, agrees; the 0-1 and 1-2 split did not). The counts here are fixed by a test; the earlier entry is left as written.

**Recorded settings per repeat.** Equal across the three repeats in every field the logs carry: arm `rrf`, model, configuration hash, temperature 0.0, seed 0, budget 8,000, template version, first stage, the day; the thinking level (`low`) and the output limit (4,096) are recorded once in `meta.json` for the whole job, not per call, so a per-call difference could not show; thinking tokens per repeat are 76,672, 67,557, 60,119. The prompt and request hashes of the first attempt are equal across the repeats for 327 of 327 instances. Ordering: the call log shows each instance's three repeats back to back in the order 0, 1, 2, for all 327 instances (the order of rows in `results.jsonl` differs between repeats and is not the run order). **Cache state differs:** first attempts with any cached tokens, repeat 0: 0 of 327, repeat 1: 300 (91.7%), repeat 2: 274 (83.8%); cached share of prompt tokens 18.9, 68.7, 63.2%. Also different: one 5xx retry in repeat 1, calls ending at the output limit 60, 79, 62, finish reason `RECITATION` 3, 5, 3. The first-attempt reply text (hash) is identical for 82 of 327 between repeats 0 and 1, 111 between 0 and 2, 294 between 1 and 2, with identical requests; repeat 0 is the only uncached repeat and is also the first in position, so cache state and position are confounded. No cause is claimed.

**Power simulation re-run.** `rq3_power_noise` lets each later run repeat the previous outcome with probability `s` (0.635, the pilot's share of identical reply texts) and otherwise draw afresh. The measured quantity is a resolution agreement, which includes the agreement that instances nearly always or never solved produce without any repeating, so it cannot be set equal to `s`; D162: the primary re-run **calibrates `s`** so that the model's pooled agreement over the three pairs equals the measured 94.09% (two pairs one position apart, one two apart; `ril/analysis/rq3_run_agreement.py`), at the measured resolved rate 39.96% and for each heterogeneity kappa of the grid. Result: **`s` = 0.806 at kappa 1 and 0.881 at kappa 4**. The direct substitution (`s` = 0.941) is run beside it as an upper bound on run dependence. Everything else is the stored simulation (N = 327, k = 1, 2, 3, kappa 1 and 4, arm effect 0 to 10 points, independent arms given the instance, seed 0, 500 datasets per cell, 2,000 permutations). The measured rate 40% is outside the old grid (10, 20, 30%), so a 40% row is added to all three scenarios. N = 232 is not re-run (N = 327 is the confirmatory set since D111 and D120). With `s` = 0.635 and the same seed the first three rates reproduce the stored table cell for cell (0 mismatches of 18).

Minimum detectable difference at power 0.8, in points (>10: not reached on the grid). Columns: old `s` = 0.635 / calibrated `s` / direct `s` = 0.941.

| rate | kappa | k = 1 | k = 2 | k = 3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10% | 1 | 5.48 / 5.48 / 5.48 | 4.91 / 5.16 / 5.38 | 4.44 / 4.88 / 5.24 |
| 10% | 4 | 7.06 / 6.99 / 7.06 | 6.05 / 6.51 / 6.58 | 5.53 / 6.22 / 6.53 |
| 20% | 1 | 7.23 / 7.09 / 7.23 | 5.76 / 6.41 / 6.16 | 5.57 / 5.88 / 6.75 |
| 20% | 4 | 8.65 / 8.84 / 8.65 | 7.66 / 8.06 / 8.38 | 7.14 / 7.96 / 8.38 |
| 30% | 1 | 7.51 / 7.66 / 7.51 | 6.99 / 7.09 / 7.44 | 6.14 / 7.07 / 7.23 |
| 30% | 4 | 9.65 / >10 / 9.65 | 8.76 / 9.29 / 9.35 | 7.67 / 9.00 / 9.02 |
| 40% (new) | 1 | 8.18 / 7.88 / 8.18 | 7.12 / 7.65 / 7.91 | 6.79 / 7.33 / 7.59 |
| 40% (new) | 4 | 9.75 / >10 / 9.75 | 9.00 / 9.63 / 9.58 | 8.49 / 9.37 / 9.85 |

At k = 3, N = 327: old 4.4 to 7.7 points over rates 10 to 30%; calibrated 4.9 to 9.0; direct 5.2 to 9.0. At the measured rate of 40%: old 6.8 to 8.5, calibrated 7.3 to 9.4, direct 7.6 to 9.9. The gain of three runs over one falls from about 1.0 to 2.0 points (old) to about 0.6 to 1.2 (calibrated). **Noise floor:** at k = 1 `s` has no effect, yet the calibrated column differs from the old one by up to 0.3 points (and 9.65 against >10 at the grid edge), because the calibrated runs use a separate random stream per kappa; differences of 0.3 points or less between columns are not distinguishable. A difference of 4 points or less stays undetectable in every cell.

**What the calibration does not settle.** (1) The pooled agreement identifies one combination of `s` and kappa, not both: with `s` = 0 the model reproduces 94.09% at kappa about 0.14, and 298 of 327 instances (91.1%) are unanimous, so the data point to instances that are almost always or never solved; the grid's kappa 1 and 4 reach the measured agreement only with `s` of 0.8 to 0.9. A simulation at kappa near 0.14 was not run (the effect-shift routine would need a change to reach the stated effect at such shapes), so the minimum detectable differences at the measured heterogeneity are not known here. (2) The calibration treats the three repeat positions as exchangeable; the data do not (repeats 1 and 2 agree at 97.9%, repeat 0 with either at 91 to 93%). (3) The measured same-edit-text share (pooled 415 of 945 = 0.44; 0.24, 0.27, 0.81 by pair) was not used. (4) The rate, kappa, effect and independence of arms remain assumptions; the resolved rate is now measured for one arm only (39.96%). The power section of `docs/RQ3_PILOT.md` and the figures the report quotes from it (4.4 to 7.7 points) are the old ones and were not changed; whether they are replaced is for the lead.

**Dead ends.** None.

## E-REPORT-DRAFT-5: report pass 5, every slot the records support (RQ2 final contrasts, the one look, calibrated power, limitations, cost of the graded runs, web-source rule, proofreading). Writing, 2026-11-14 (decision D165)

Status: **done; 24 pages, 5 open slots (all wait for E-RQ3-AGENT), no model call, no cluster job.** Evidence class: every figure is read from a stored file through a generator (`ril/analysis/report_tables.py`) or quoted from a named entry in a source comment; no new measurement.

**Gate for RQ2 numbers.** The first read of E-RQ2-FINAL on main had point estimates outside their stated intervals; the table was being recomputed (DEV-044). RQ2 numbers were written only after the correction was merged (commit `e14a8b6`): intervals are the unadjusted cluster-robust t intervals with the bootstrap beside them and the Holm-adjusted p as the decision, read from `results/tables/rq2_final_contrasts.csv` (generators `rq2_final_confirmatory`, `rq2_final_exploratory`). The two contrasts against RAW on the executable 377 (L2 nDCG@10 +0.82, t [0.06, 1.57]; L3 Recall@100 +0.67, t [0.00, 1.34]) are reported with both the unadjusted interval and the adjusted p of 0.15 and called inconclusive. No confirmatory contrast is rejected. The abstract, contributions and conclusion carry only RQ2 statements from this record; their RQ3 sentences stay slots. After main gained the lead's rulings and the seed-101 sensitivity (E-RQ2-SENS-SEED101, exploratory, D164), the report also carries it beside the registered result: a generated table of the six confirmatory contrasts with each seed left out (`rq2_final_leave_seed`), stating that the one contrast rejected without seed 101 (L3 Recall@100 against RAW, p 0.039) is not stable to the seed average and is not evidence of an effect; threats T24 and T29 follow the rulings (six off-card runs kept, DEV-045). The decision id of this pass is D165 because D164 is the lead's rulings.

**Filled.** (1) RQ2 confirmatory and exploratory contrasts (E-RQ2-FINAL). (2) The one look: denominators 179 / 177 (eight repositories), 320 / 316 (L2) and 224 (L3) on the executable 377, 717 rows per model, the pinned rule for queries without a positive. (3) Power: the earlier table relabelled as the earlier assumption, a new generated table with the repeat probability calibrated to the measured agreement (E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT: 4.9 to 9.0 points at three runs, 7.3 to 9.4 at the measured 40%), the measured baseline rate 39.96% (descriptive, one arm), adjacent cached repeats stated as not independent. (4) Threats and limitations: rows T24 to T29 (training hardware, queries without positives, unchecked test-list equality, contamination exposure, one-shot repair stage, second teacher not run), T16 and T17 rewritten (single provider and model, thinking low, output limit 4,096 with 11.9% of check calls and 15.5% of the untrained arm's calls at it), a paragraph on the graded runs. (5) Tokens and cost: generated table `rq3_cost` from the stored call logs, cost 9.875 USD for the arm, 1.690 USD for the model check, 11.565 of 50 USD. (6) Web source: see D165. (7) Proofreading: stale statements removed (nothing trained, no model called, no resolved rate, dense-teacher cell runs later, stores not evaluated), the UTBoost sentence fixed ("54.2% of them" read as the 26 instances; the paper's figure is the share of the 500 whose stored test lists the improved parser changed), numbers of every `knowledge/papers` comment re-found in the cited note (one mismatch, my own new power line, checked by hand).

**Honest sentences in place of slots (not measured):** audited noise rate and reader agreement (E-LABEL-AUDIT-SHEET: no pair read), sibling-noise decision (E-PILOT-NOISE-SIB), stacked expansion on Verified-six and the third development repository (E-GRAPH-L3: needs an encoding pass), reranker and end-to-end dense latency row (ADR-009, E-LAT-STAGES), longer-schedule sensitivity (DEV-039), budget in the agent model's tokens (provider counts now in `rq3_cost`; contexts stay in approximate tokens).

**Not done.** The 45 cited papers were not re-read from their PDFs in this pass: 29 notes have extracted text on this machine and were not re-extracted; 17 (the pass-4 and earlier-scaffold papers) have none stored locally. What was done is mechanical: every cited key has a note whose header is `read: full`, the one partly read note is stated in the report, and the numbers of each cited sentence were re-found in the note. A claim-by-claim re-reading against the PDFs remains open. The web announcement was re-fetched and its claims re-found in the capture.

**Dead ends.** None.

## E-RQ2-SENS-SEED101: the final analysis without seed 101, exploratory, 2026-11-14 (decision D164)

Status: **done; exploratory, CPU only, outside the registered tables; the registered result of E-RQ2-FINAL is unchanged and stays the reported one.** Evidence class: measured (stored rows), exploratory. Authorised by the lead on 2026-11-14 because the five mined models of seed 101 trained on an RTX 4070 while the other ten trained on the requested RTX 2080 Ti (E-RQ2-DEV-ANALYSIS, D160).

**What was run.** The stored final rows (E-RQ2-FINAL, sha256 `74ff26da...`) and runs table with seed 101 removed (7,170 rows, 10 runs), through the same module and defaults (`scripts/rq2_analysis.py`, seed 0, 2,000 draws, alpha 0.05, delta 1.5, same dev-analysis id), `--source-kind pilot` so that the output cannot enter a headline. No rule, family, set or contrast was changed. Files `results/rq2_final/sensitivity_seed101/`: rows (sha256 `ab002fed...`), runs (`66266e78...`), summary (`9426efb7...`, analysis hash `0b4458a9e562`). Nothing was scored; no GPU.

**Result (estimates in points, unadjusted cluster-robust t interval, Holm-adjusted p; three seeds as registered against seeds 100 and 102 only):**
- Eight repositories, L2 nDCG@10: vs RAW -0.14, t [-1.50, +1.22], p 1.00 against +0.02, t [-1.22, +1.25], p 1.00; vs subsample -0.02, p 1.00 against +0.19, t [-0.62, +0.99], p 1.00.
- Executable 377, L2 nDCG@10: vs RAW +0.82, t [+0.06, +1.57], p 0.15 against +0.32, t [-0.45, +1.09], p 1.00; vs subsample +0.26, p 1.00 against +0.18, t [-0.54, +0.90], p 1.00.
- Executable 377, L3 Recall@100: **vs RAW +0.67, t [+0.00, +1.34], p 0.15 against +0.75, t [+0.24, +1.26], p 0.039, rejected by Holm in the sensitivity**; vs subsample +0.02, p 1.00 against -0.12, t [-0.61, +0.37], p 1.00.
- One confirmatory contrast changes decision, in the direction of an effect: FILTER-BM25 against RAW on executable-377 L3 Recall@100. The other five stay not rejected.
- Exploratory contrasts: the sensitivity rejects 1 of 12 (CURRICULUM minus subsample at executable-377 L3 Recall@100, -1.11, p 0.0001); the registered analysis rejects 3 (that one, CURRICULUM minus RAW at the same cell, and CURRICULUM minus subsample at L2 nDCG@10). CURRICULUM stays lower in every cell; WEIGHT is not distinguishable from RAW.
- Per-seed estimates of the contrast that changed (stored, from E-RQ2-FINAL): seed 100 -0.45, seed 101 +0.51, seed 102 +1.95. Seed 101 sits between the other two; the estimate moves by 0.08 points when it is dropped, and the adjusted p moves from 0.15 to 0.039.

**What this can show.** That the one near-miss confirmatory contrast of the registered analysis is not stable to the choice of which seeds are averaged: it lies on both sides of the 0.05 line depending on whether one of three seeds is included. That excluding the off-card seed reverses no estimate larger than 0.1 points in absolute value and creates no harmful contrast. The largest shift of an estimate that carries a decision is 0.50 points (executable-377 L2 nDCG@10 against RAW, +0.82 to +0.32); the largest of any of the 36 contrasts is 0.96 points (eight repositories, Recall@100, FILTER-BM25 against RAW, which carries no decision).

**What it cannot show.** That seed 101's hardware caused any difference: seed 101's own estimate is not an outlier on the contrast that changed, and the sensitivity does not isolate hardware from seed. It is not a replacement for the registered analysis and is not evidence of an effect: it is one of several seed subsets that could have been chosen, with no multiplicity adjustment across them, and the interval of either analysis carries query and repository sampling, not retraining variance (DEV-040), so dropping a seed changes the per-query means without a measure of the variation between retrainings. The other two leave-one-seed-out analyses were run afterwards (addendum below), and I did not investigate why the adjusted p falls by a factor of four while the estimate moves by 0.08. **Reading to report:** the registered result (no confirmatory rejection; the contrast at executable-377 L3 Recall@100 against RAW is inconclusive, p 0.15) is the result; the sensitivity result is reported beside it, with its direction, as a statement that this contrast is fragile at three seeds.

**Not claimed.** Any effect of FILTER-BM25 supervision; any effect of the card; anything beyond the 15 mined models and the two sealed sets.

Decision **D164** (the lead's rulings, 2026-11-14; no design choice by me): D160 confirmed as a placement fault with future GPU requests naming memory or card type; the six off-card runs kept with hardware recorded; the dense-teacher measurement and the SweRank cell not run (DEV-045); the pinned reading on queries without positives kept; the eight E-RQ2-ANALYSIS questions closed; the seed-101 sensitivity authorised as exploratory (this entry). Evidence class: measured (spend, hardware records), reasoned (the rulings). Cost / change my mind: the cell can be run later while budget remains; a re-run of the six runs would cost about 2.5 GPU-hours.

**Counts of Holm rejections.** Registered: 0 confirmatory, 3 exploratory. Without seed 100: 0 and 3 (a different three). Without seed 101: 1 and 1. Without seed 102: 0 and 1.

**What the table shows.** (1) The registered three-seed result is the one reported: no confirmatory contrast is rejected. (2) Across the three leave-one-out choices the confirmatory contrast at executable-377 L3 Recall@100, FILTER-BM25 against RAW, takes estimates from +0.03 to +1.23 and adjusted p from 0.04 to 1.00: rejected only when seed 101 is dropped (+0.75, p 0.039), not when seed 100 is dropped (+1.23, wide interval, p 0.33) or seed 102 (+0.03, p 1.00). That spread is the point: the contrast is not stable to which seeds are averaged, and a rejection under one subset is not evidence of an effect. Its positive estimate depends on seed 102 (+1.95; seeds 100 and 101 are -0.45 and +0.51). (3) Executable-377 L2 nDCG@10, FILTER-BM25 against RAW, is positive in every subset (+0.32 to +1.28) and nearest to rejection when seed 100 is dropped (p 0.052); it is rejected in none. (4) The other four confirmatory contrasts are not rejected in any subset. (5) CURRICULUM minus subsample at executable-377 L3 Recall@100 is rejected in the registered analysis and in all three leave-one-out analyses (-0.86 to -1.26); the other CURRICULUM rejections of the registered analysis (L2 nDCG@10 against the subsample at the executable 377; L3 Recall@100 against RAW) do not hold in every subset, and one further contrast (eight repositories, L2 nDCG@10, CURRICULUM minus subsample) is rejected only when seed 100 is dropped. These are exploratory and carry no claim.

**What this can and cannot show.** It shows the size of the choice that three seeds leave open: with two of three seeds, decisions and estimates move by amounts that the intervals (query and repository sampling, no retraining variance, DEV-040) do not express. It cannot show that any subset is the better estimate, that the card of seed 101 mattered, or that FILTER-BM25 has an effect on any metric. The three subsets are one family with no multiplicity adjustment; the rejection without seed 101 is one of three such looks and is not to be quoted without the other two.

## E-REPORT-DRAFT-6: report pass 6, full read of the sixteen cited papers that had no extracted text, and a check of the seed-sensitivity table against its entries. Writing and reading, 2026-11-14 (decision D166)

Status: **done; 25 pages with the engine available here (the same sources before this pass also build to 25 there), 5 open slots (all wait for E-RQ3-AGENT), no model call, no cluster job, no run.** Evidence class: read in full (sixteen papers), measured (the table is read from stored summaries).

**Papers.** Of the 45 cited papers, 29 had extracted text on the working machine and 16 did not: Agentless v2, SWE-agent v3, OpenHands v3, the contrastive confidence regularizer (v2), the noisy pair corrector, curriculum learning for retrieval distillation, Penha and Hauff, ADAM (v2), Promptagator, GloFND (v2), self-distillation with adaptive margins (v2), the MS MARCO fine-tuning study, MM-IssueLoc, ARHN, UTBoost and Skip the Compressor. (The previous pass counted 17; the sixteen are all the cited keys without a stored text, and the web announcement has its archive capture.) Each PDF was fetched again at the version its note names, its page count equals the note's (25, 118, 38, 14, 13, 5, 15, 13, 21, 18, 11, 7, 22, 10, 13, 24), no extracted page is blank, and `scripts/check_paper_note.py` passes on all sixteen (every number of every "may quote" table found again). The text was then read page by page, body and appendices; reference-list pages were checked for the entries the text cites. Two headers are qualified: SWE-agent (pages 45 to 71 now read in full, only the numbered source-code listing lines of pages 72 to 116 unread, nothing rests on them; it was pages 45 to 116) and MM-IssueLoc (pages 15 to 22, example figures and prompt templates, scanned only; nothing rests on them). The UTBoost leaderboard tables of the appendix were read in part. Every header now carries the PDF SHA-256 and the date; the files are kept outside the tracked tree. `knowledge/papers/README.md`, `docs/EVAL_API.md` and an addendum to ADR-007 carry the narrowed SWE-agent scope.

**Sentences of the report changed because of the papers (seven; every other sentence citing the sixteen was found supported, including each number and attribution):**
1. Agentless: "Every cell of its tables is one run" became "Its tables report no repeated runs": the paper reports no repeats but does not say that each cell is one run.
2. Loop costs: "about 1.6 and 1.7 dollars per Lite instance" became about 1.7 for both, with the two figures (1.67 for SWE-agent, counted over resolved instances only; 1.72 for OpenHands, averaging not stated); 1.67 had been rounded down.
3. Curriculum learning: "Curriculum and self-paced selection for retrieval distillation [CL-DRD, Penha and Hauff, ADAM]" was wrong for Penha and Hauff (conversation response ranking with a BERT ranker, no distillation); the sentence now names each work for what it does.
4. Promptagator: "hurt the two smallest" became "hurt others, among them the two with the fewest generated queries": the paper says the filter hurts "such as NFCorpus and SciFact" and that these have the fewest generated queries; 8 of 11 improve, so a third dataset also did not.
5. GloFND: "(image data)" became "(image and image-text data)": the warm-up is also used in its image-text runs.
6. Self-distillation: "using the encoder's own similarity as the training target peaked after about half of the data" now names the variant (adaptive margins), the one for which the paper states it.
7. Skip the Compressor: the lead of 6.3 to 12.7 points is over the best of the three compressors (6.3) and the best of the three retrievers (9.3 and 12.7), with two models, the selection being made by the model under test; a trained retriever of whole functions came within two points (0.7 and 1.7). The old wording read as a lead over each baseline.

**Sensitivity table.** The six confirmatory rows of `results/tables/rq2_final_leave_one_seed_out.csv` (12 contrasts, 48 cells) were compared cell by cell (estimate, Holm-adjusted p, rejection, per-seed estimates) with the four stored summaries (`results/rq2_final/final/final_analysis_summary.json` and the three of `results/rq2_final/sensitivity_seed101/`): 0 differences. The generator output (`rq2_final_leave_seed`, all 28 fragments) is identical to the committed fragments. The leave-one-out analyses without seed 100 and without seed 102 are recorded (E-RQ2-SENS-SEED101 addendum), so no row is a placeholder. The numbers in the report's sentences (+0.75 with p 0.039 without seed 101, +1.23 with p 0.33 without seed 100, +0.03 with p 1.00 without seed 102, registered +0.67 with p 0.15, per-seed -0.45, +0.51, +1.95) equal the stored values. One sentence was added: the estimate runs from +0.03 to +1.23 points and the adjusted p from 0.039 to 1.00 over the three choices, and that spread is the finding, not any one subset; the report already stated that the registered three-seed result is the reported one and that a rejection under one of three subsets is not evidence of an effect.

**Not done.** Reference lists were not read entry by entry (all sixteen), pages 72 to 116 of the SWE-agent trajectories lack their source-listing lines, pages 15 to 22 of MM-IssueLoc were scanned, and the 29 notes that already had text were checked mechanically (every number found again) but their sentences in the report were not re-compared in this pass. The web announcement was not re-fetched.

**Dead ends.** The first fetch of the ARHN PDF in the batch returned a partial file; the complete file (10 pages) came on the retry, which is the one used. None otherwise.

## E-RQ3-AGENT: resolved rate of the three trained arms against the untrained arm, three adjacent repeats on the 327 confirmatory instances. Measured, pre-registered analysis, 2026-11-14 (decision D167)

Status: **done; one primary contrast, estimates for the rest, no interpretation beyond the rules.** Evidence class: measured (config hash `0fe87a17bf1b41873ebedb03c09adcab2a030ce3d7eb3e8159a189da280be46a` on every call of every arm, equal to the selection of E-RQ3-MODEL-CHECK-RESULT; seed 0, temperature 0, thinking level low, output limit 4,096, arms at 8,000 tokens, runs 0, 1, 2 sent back to back per instance, account A). Files: `results/rq3_driver/trained_runs/gemini-3.8-flash/<arm>_runs0-2/{results/meta.json,results/results.jsonl,log/calls.jsonl}` (981 rows each), the untrained arm of E-RQ3-UNTRAINED-RUNS, `results/rq3_driver/remaining_arms/` (analysis tables and `arm_summary.json` with the SHA-256 of every input), `results/rq3_driver/trained_runs/shared_spend_ledger.json`.

**Reading taken (stated first).** "The three remaining arms of the analysis plan" is read as the three trained arms at training seed 100: **RAW** (the primary pair against the untrained arm, D150), **FILTER-BM25** (RQ2's confirmatory cell) and the **size-matched subsample** `mined_sub` (RQ2's confirmatory control; third in the protocol order of the plan, D151). The fourth planned arm is the untrained `rrf`, graded in E-RQ3-UNTRAINED-RUNS. WEIGHT, CURRICULUM, the other seeds and the SweRank cell are not run: 11.41 USD remain and one arm costs about 9. Each arm is one retained model (one training seed), so the contrasts carry the variation of one training run per arm and nothing about training-seed variance.

**Preconditions.** Spend authorisation file dated 2026-11-12 (total 50 USD); limits file has no UNSET value; the key file on A is mode 600 (not read, not printed); the retained scores of the three seed-100 models verified (checkpoint hash, every file hash, 377 members, 224 with dense_L3 per DEV-038; copy from C to A of the score arrays and 2 KB manifests only, 190 MB; the check job and the contexts job ran on A); gate A met when the scoring job on C had finished the RAW, FILTER-BM25 and subsample models of seed 100 (the final-sets job of seed 100, still running for the other models; the jobs of seeds 101 and 102 were not waited for). The scoring of other seeds and models continued in another session and was not touched.

**Contexts (CPU, account A).** The check of the untrained path first: 30 of 30 stored contexts rebuilt byte-identical (`differ` 0; job 77750). Then 377 of 377 contexts per model at both budgets (job 77893, 1 h 17 min, four shards), each arm RRF of the stored BM25 scores and the model's scores at the frozen k 100 (D152); prepare (job 78233, 35 s): 377 of 377 prompts per arm pass the screen, 0 rejected, pseudonymised prefixes identical, issue text equal to the table's for 320 of 320, 327 confirmatory and 50 held-out ids. Dead ends, none of which made a call: job 77416 failed after 2 min (the subset file was not under the work directory; copied), job 77750 failed after the check passed (four shards wrote the same temporary file for the score-source record; `write_json_atomic` now uses a per-process name, `ril/evaluation/rq3_trained.py`), arm jobs 78235 to 78237 failed at start (wrong prepared path), resubmitted as 78244 to 78246.

**Spend control (new apparatus, D167).** The runner reserves the worst case of each call in a locked JSON ledger (`SharedSpend` in `ril/agent/providers/spend.py`: exclusive `flock`, atomic rename, cap fixed by the first job at 38.4 USD so that 11.565 + 38.4 = 49.965 stays below 50, a restart clears only its own reservation, a failed call releases its reservation); each arm also had its own cap of 12 USD and a stop when more than 10% of final replies are unusable from 50 units on; `--expect-config-sha256` refuses a start when the run's hash differs. The hash input names the arm as the comparator `rrf` for every arm (the arm is a prompt label, not a setting; the selected hash was computed with this name; a test reproduces `0fe87a17bf1b…`). Final ledger: committed 27.021 USD, nothing reserved. No arm reached a cap or the unusable stop; all three stopped `complete`; no node fault.

**Queue and start times.** Jobs 78244 (RAW) and 78245 (FILTER-BM25) started 12:49 and ran 5 h 38 min; the subsample job 78246 was submitted at 12:49 with them and waited behind the per-user CPU allowance until 18:27 (5 h 38 min), then ran 4 h 14 min. Account C could not have run it without the key, driver, executor data, repository cache and subsample prompts (checked read-only); the lead ruled that all arms stay on A so that the environment is the same for every arm.

**Summary (327 confirmatory instances x 3 repeats; the 50 held-out instances are not in it).**
