Discordant instances (resolved in some repeat in exactly one of two arms, any-of-3): RAW against untrained 30 and 18, FILTER-BM25 against untrained 28 and 23, subsample against untrained 24 and 24 (`rq3_results_discordance.csv`).

**What the data do and do not show.** The primary contrast is not distinguishable from zero: +2.45 points, interval [-0.82, +5.91], permutation p = 0.177. FILTER-BM25 and the subsample are not distinguishable from the untrained arm either, and FILTER-BM25 is not distinguishable from RAW or from the subsample. One estimate has an interval that excludes zero, subsample below RAW (-3.16 [-6.22, -0.10]); it is one of five estimates, not adjusted for the number of contrasts, its upper end is 0.10 points from zero, and it is not read as a finding. The same holds for the any-of-3 reading (subsample below RAW, [-7.03, -0.31]). **Power:** the stored simulation gives a minimum detectable difference at power 0.8 of about 7 to 9.5 points at the measured 40% rate (7.3 to 9.4 with the repeat probability calibrated to the measured agreement, N = 327, k = 3; 4.9 to 9.0 at rates 10 to 30%; E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT); every observed difference (at most 3.16 points) lies below it, and the half-width of the primary interval is 3.4 points. A true difference of the size the pilot assumed to matter (4 points or less) would not have been detected in any cell of that grid. **Adjacent repeats are not independent runs:** repeats 2 and 3 of an instance are sent right after the first, the provider's prompt cache serves them (67.8 to 68.7% of prompt tokens against 16 to 18% for repeat 0), and the three repeats agree on the resolved status of an instance in 91 to 98% of pairs (E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT, untrained arm); the interval treats the instances as the sampling unit and the repeats only reduce within-instance noise. **Truncation:** 13.7 to 15.5% of calls end at the 4,096 output limit in every arm (untrained 15.5%, RAW 13.7%, FILTER-BM25 14.2%, subsample 14.0%), so a share of replies is cut in the same way for all arms; unusable final replies are 2.0 to 2.6%, far below the 10% stop. **Scope:** each trained arm is one model from one training seed (100); absolute resolved rates are not agent-capability claims (benchmark contamination inflates them identically across arms); Python only; the executable subset of Verified only (D106); the retrieval-versus-reasoning failure breakdown of this run is not made here (`rq3_results_apply_failure_classes.csv` holds the apply-failure classes).

**Plan and design lines.** The analysis plan file is the generated one (`ril.agent.providers.trained_arms.build_plan`: primary RAW against untrained, estimates FILTER and subsample against untrained and against RAW) plus one estimate, FILTER-BM25 minus subsample (RQ2's second confirmatory pair), added before any contrast was computed and marked in the file. Contexts and prompts were built on account A (idle, holds the driver, executor and the stored untrained contexts for the equality check), not on C as the engineer script assumes.

**Spend against the authorisation.** Arms 8.931 (RAW) + 9.117 (FILTER-BM25) + 8.974 (subsample) = 27.021 USD (ledger and guard equal: 27.021); untrained arm 9.875; model check 1.690: **38.587 USD of 50, 11.413 USD remaining.** Cap per arm 12 USD, never reached; shared cap 38.4 USD, 27.02 used.

**Tactical record.** Account C's GPU environment lacked `yaml`; installed pyyaml into its Python 3.10 site-packages. A login-node `ensurepip` (Python 3.9) also created a `python3.9` library folder and pip scripts in that environment: additive, harmless to the 3.10 environment; removing them is left to the engineer. Not done: other seeds, WEIGHT, CURRICULUM, the 16,000-token prompts, the SweRank cell, a separate retrieval-versus-reasoning breakdown, a run of the report's power table with the measured agreement.

**Dead ends.** Described above (three job failures with no call, one race). An early reading of the flat ledger at 16:50 as a stall was wrong: the paid calls had finished and the executor was still grading.

## E-RQ3-ORACLE: resolved rate when the context is guaranteed to contain the gold-patch files (positive control). Exploratory, stated before any call, 2026-11-14 (decision D168)

Status: **done 2026-11-15 (D168): run once as written, 654 graded rows, 5.717 USD; exploratory, descriptive.** Evidence class: measured (job 80484, account A, config hash `0fe87a17bf1b`, seed 0, results under `results/rq3_driver/oracle_runs/`, analysis under `results/rq3_oracle/`).

**Why.** The registered RQ3 contrasts found no difference between arms that differ in retriever supervision (E-RQ3-AGENT). A null among arms is hard to read without knowing how much the scaffold can gain from retrieval at all. This arm is a positive control: it measures how many resolved-fraction points the scaffold gains when the assembled context contains, by construction, the files the gold patch modifies, that is, the headroom retrieval can buy. The null among the registered arms is then read against it. It is not a retriever and not part of the registered analysis.

**Direction, stated before the run.** The oracle arm is expected to resolve at least as many instances as the untrained arm. It is run once, as written; no further arm is added after seeing it; its contrasts are exploratory estimates with no multiplicity claim.

**Design.**
- *Context.* For each instance the units of the files that the gold patch modifies (pre-patch contents, from the base-commit snapshot) are placed first in the candidate list, in file-path then line order; the remaining budget is filled by the same rrf ranking as the untrained arm (same candidates, frozen k), skipping units already included. The assembly policy is the one of every other arm (first fit, unit cap 1,000 approximate tokens, blocks ordered by file and line, 8,000-token budget, approximate tokenizer), so format, ordering rule, pseudonymisation, screen and restore are those of the other arms. Where the gold files alone exceed the budget, the same cut and skip rules apply, and the instances where any unit of a gold file is cut or left out are counted and reported. A file the patch creates has no pre-patch content and contributes nothing; a modified file with no parsed units (a non-Python file) contributes nothing; both are counted.
- *Gold files.* The paths modified by the gold patch (diff headers of the benchmark table's patch field, files with a pre-patch version only), read once into a paths-only file on the cluster; the patch text is not carried into any context step.
- *No leakage.* The gold patch, the test patch and test names must never appear in a prompt. A check runs on every oracle prompt and fails the build (non-zero exit, no run job starts without a clean report): patch structure lines (diff headers, hunk headers) of the gold or test patch; runs of at least three consecutive added lines of either patch in the context text (whitespace-normalised, not in the issue text); full test identifiers of the FAIL_TO_PASS and PASS_TO_PASS lists. The same check is run on the untrained arm's prompts as a baseline for its false-positive rate.
- *Instances.* Contexts and prompts for all 377; graded on the 327 confirmatory instances; the held-out 50 are not analysed (D111).
- *Model and settings.* Exactly the selected configuration, hash `0fe87a17bf1b` (gemini-3.8-flash, thinking level low, output limit 4,096); the run refuses to start on a mismatch. k = 2 adjacent repeats (the two repeats of one instance back to back), one job, account A, the executor environment of the other arms.
- *Spend.* The lead authorised the remaining 11.413 USD of the 50 USD ceiling (38.587 spent). This arm is capped at 8.5 USD through the shared locked ledger. The run stops cleanly when the cost projected from the first 40 instances (spent per instance times 327) exceeds the cap, at more than 10% unusable final replies (from 50 units on), and on an insufficient-balance error (recorded, reported; the provider balance cannot be read from here, the guard is the only protection). No cache-hit stop. One resubmission on a node fault with that node excluded; any other failure stops the arm and is reported.

**Analysis (exploratory, descriptive).** Resolved fraction per repeat and mean; unusable and truncation rates; cost and tokens. Contrasts as estimates by the registered method (paired difference of the mean resolved fraction per instance, paired bootstrap interval, sign-flip permutation p): oracle minus untrained and oracle minus RAW, no adjustment, no decision. Retrieval-versus-reasoning view from the stored coverage rows: resolved fraction of the oracle arm among the instances where the context of the other arms contained none of the gold files. The minimum detectable difference from E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT is stated beside the estimates, with the two caveats that k is 2 here against 3 in the other arms and that adjacent repeats are not independent runs.

**Design lines added while building** (none changes what is measured).
- *Gold files* are the old paths of the `diff --git` blocks of the gold patch (a created file has no pre-patch content and is counted; 1 instance). The candidate list is the gold-file units followed by the rrf top 200, so a block's rank is its position in that list.
- *Leakage check, final form (differential).* Composition: diff and hunk header lines of the gold or test patch; runs of three consecutive added lines (at least 40 characters); full FAIL_TO_PASS and PASS_TO_PASS identifiers; run on the restored prompt, context part only, digests reported, never text. The first (strict) form flagged 238 matches in 60 of 377 oracle prompts and 1,371 matches in 339 of 1,131 untrained prompts: text that already stood in the repository (existing tests are the PASS_TO_PASS set; patches re-add lines already present). A strict check cannot tell a leak from that, so the gate became differential: a match counts only when its text is absent from the base-commit version of the file whose block holds it (read with `git show` at the instance's base commit), and every non-blank context line must come from that file (`not_prepatch`; truncation markers excepted). Result: 0 findings in 377 oracle prompts (238 matches all pre-existing) and 0 in the 1,131 untrained prompts (1,371 pre-existing). The strict report is kept beside the final one. The check is a tripwire on the pre-patch snapshot, not a proof about the model: the context cannot hold patch text because it is built from a checkout with no patch applied.
- *Projection stop* is evaluated at the first instance boundary after 40 instances are logged (projected 6.14 USD from 0.751 USD for 40 instances, under the cap; no stop).
- *Insufficient balance:* a 429 naming prepayment or depleted credits stops the run as an insufficient balance; the generic quota text does not. Not triggered.

### Results (exploratory; descriptive; nothing adjusted, nothing decided)

**Run.** Job 80484 (account A, 2 h 14 min), stopped `complete`: 654 rows (327 instances x 2 adjacent repeats), 0 errors, 14 unusable (2.1%), 100 of 756 calls at the output limit (13.2%), 5.717 USD (projected 6.14), 6.80M input / 0.69M output / 2.90M cached tokens (cache share 73% on the second repeat). Slurm records an out-of-memory kill event after the driver had logged completion (peak 8.39 GB of the 8 GB request, as the other arms at 7.5 to 8.4 GB); `no_test_output` rows are 9 of 654 (1.4%) against 1.2 to 1.6% in the other arms, so no effect on grading is visible.

**Resolved fraction (327 confirmatory instances, exact reading; interval = percentile bootstrap of the instance means).**

| Arm | Repeats | Resolved fraction | 95% interval | By repeat |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Oracle | 2 | 45.11% | [39.91, 50.15] | 44.65 / 45.57 |
| Untrained rrf | 3 | 39.96% | [34.96, 45.06] | 40.67 / 39.14 / 40.06 |
| RAW | 3 | 42.41% | [37.51, 47.60] | 43.12 / 42.51 / 41.59 |
| FILTER-BM25 | 3 | 41.59% | [36.39, 46.69] | 42.20 / 41.28 / 41.28 |
| Subsample | 3 | 39.25% | [34.35, 44.24] | 37.92 / 40.06 / 39.76 |

The tolerant reading gives the same numbers for every arm.

**Contrasts (oracle minus arm; paired difference of the mean resolved fraction per instance, paired bootstrap interval, sign-flip permutation p; unadjusted; N = 327).**

| Contrast | k form | Difference (points) | 95% interval | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Oracle minus untrained | own k (2 vs 3) | +5.15 | [+0.61, +9.73] | 0.035 |
| Oracle minus untrained | matched k (2 vs 2) | +5.20 | [+0.61, +9.79] | 0.038 |
| Oracle minus RAW | own k (2 vs 3) | +2.70 | [-1.98, +7.49] | 0.277 |
| Oracle minus RAW | matched k (2 vs 2) | +2.29 | [-2.29, +7.03] | 0.372 |

FILTER-BM25 (+3.52, [-1.12, +8.46], p 0.153) and the subsample (+5.86, [+1.22, +10.75], p 0.017) are in `rq3_oracle_contrasts.csv`; they are not a selected contrast. Eight rows in all, none adjusted for multiplicity.

**Gold files in the oracle context (the 327).** The gold files were cut or partly left out of the 8,000-token budget in 208 instances (units cut in 113, a unit left out in 174; 246 of the 377 in all): the oracle arm is guaranteed to hold the files that the gold patch modifies only as far as the budget allows, so it is a calibration of headroom, not a ceiling on what any context could give. One instance's patch creates a file with no pre-patch content, one modified file has no parsed unit.

**What the registered arms' contexts held.** No gold-patch file in the context: untrained 59, RAW 48, FILTER-BM25 51, subsample 50 of 327; every gold file in the context: 250, 256, 255, 257.

**Retrieval versus reasoning.** Among the 37 instances where the untrained and RAW contexts held none of the gold files, the oracle arm resolved 28.4% against 3.6% (untrained) and 5.4% (RAW): +24.8 points [+12.6, +38.3] and +23.0 [+11.7, +35.6]. Among the 34 where all four registered arms missed every gold file: oracle 27.9%, the four arms 3.9 to 7.8%. These sets are chosen on a retrieval outcome, so the difference there says what a guaranteed gold file is worth when retrieval failed; it does not estimate the average effect.

**Minimum detectable difference** (E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT, N = 327, rate 40%, kappa 1): k = 2 (this arm) 7.12 assumed, 7.65 calibrated, 7.91 measured points; k = 3 (the registered arms) 6.79, 7.33, 7.59; at kappa 4, 9.0 / 9.63 / 9.58 for k = 2. The oracle-minus-untrained difference (+5.2) is below all of these, so a difference of that size is not reliably detectable at this sample size and the interval, which lies just above zero, is one of eight unadjusted estimates.

**What this shows and does not show.** The direction stated beforehand held: the oracle arm resolved more than the untrained arm (45.1% against 40.0%), and not distinguishably more than RAW. Putting the gold files first gains about 5 points over the untrained context and about 2.5 over RAW (interval including zero), so the measured headroom from supplying the gold files is about twice the largest gap between registered arms (RAW minus untrained +2.45), and on the instances where the registered arms held no gold file the oracle arm resolves about a quarter more instances (28% against 4 to 8%). It does not show that better retrieval would produce an effect of that size for a trained retriever (the oracle is not a retriever), does not rank retrievers, and does not support a claim about the registered contrasts, which stay not distinguishable from zero. k is 2 against 3, and adjacent repeats are not independent runs, so the repeat noise is understated for this arm; the matched-k contrasts are the sensitivity for that. Absolute resolved rates are not capability claims.

**Spend.** 5.717 USD (ledger and guard equal: 5.717; cap 8.5 USD, never reached). Total **44.304 USD of 50; 5.696 USD of the 11.413 authorised remain.** Shared ledger: 32.738 committed against the 38.4 cap.

**Dead ends and incidents.** (1) The first strict leakage check failed on matches that already stood in the repository; replaced by the differential gate above (no loosening of what counts as a leak: text absent from the pre-patch file). (2) Run job 80457 refused to start: the expected hash in the brief had a wrong last character (a transcription slip) against the recorded `0fe87a17bf1b`; no call was made and the ledger was unchanged; resubmitted once as job 80484 with the full 64-character hash (`0fe87a17bf1b41873ebedb03c09adcab2a030ce3d7eb3e8159a189da280be46a`) that every registered arm's result file carries. (3) A foreground poll of a long job timed out into the background; waits use notifications. (4) `ruff format .` at repository level touched unrelated files (reverted); only the files of the task are formatted. Not done: other contexts or budgets for the oracle, more repeats, any arm added after seeing this one.

## E-RQ3-FLOOR: resolved rate when the context holds no retrieved code (floor reference). Exploratory, stated before any call, 2026-11-15 (decision D169)

Status: **run once as written 2026-11-15 (D169) and stopped by its own projection rule after 40 of 327 instances (0.786 USD); exploratory, descriptive, partial.** Evidence class: measured (job 80738, account A, config hash `0fe87a17bf1b`, seed 0, results under `results/rq3_driver/floor_runs/`, analysis under `results/rq3_floor/`). The stated expectation did not hold: the floor is not near zero.

**Why.** The arms of E-RQ3-AGENT and the oracle of E-RQ3-ORACLE sit on a ladder: none, retrieved (untrained, RAW, FILTER-BM25, subsample), oracle. The bottom rung has not been measured. A no-retrieval arm gives the floor of that ladder and a positive control that the harness can see a large effect: if the floor is far below the untrained arm, the harness resolves differences of that size, so the null among the registered arms is not a blind harness.

**Expectation, stated before the run.** The resolved fraction is far below the untrained arm (39.96%), near zero, because an edit whose SEARCH text must match the repository character for character cannot be written without the code. If the result is not near zero it is reported as it is. The arm is run once, as written; no further arm is added after seeing it; its contrasts are exploratory estimates with no multiplicity claim.

**Design.**
- *Context.* None. The prompt is built by the same function, with the same header, template version and instructions as every other arm; the section after the "Repository excerpts" heading is empty.
- *Path filter.* The reply parser rejects an edit block whose path is not among the files the context showed (`path_not_in_context`). With an empty context that list is empty, so every reply would be unusable by construction. For this arm the prepared items carry `paths: null` (no path filter); every other check (format, exact application, grading) is unchanged. Recorded as a design line: without it the floor would measure the filter, not the model.
- *Instances.* Prompts for the 377; graded on the 327 confirmatory instances (the held-out 50 are not graded, D111).
- *Model and settings.* Exactly the selected configuration (gemini-3.8-flash, thinking level low, output limit 4,096, temperature 0). The 64-character configuration hash is read from the stored result files of the other arms and passed to the run, never typed; the run refuses to start on a mismatch.
- *Repeats.* k = 1. One job, account A, the executor environment of the other arms.
- *Leak check.* The differential leak check of E-RQ3-ORACLE runs on every floor prompt and fails the build (non-zero exit; the run job refuses a directory without a clean report for all 377 prompts). With no context it is vacuous by construction; it is run anyway.
- *Spend.* The lead authorised what remains of the 50 USD ceiling (44.304 spent, 5.696 left). The provider balance is about 4.9 USD and cannot be read from here, so this arm is capped at 2.5 USD through the shared locked ledger. The run stops cleanly when the cost projected from the first 40 instances (spent per instance times 327) exceeds 2.5 USD, at more than 10% unusable final replies (from 50 units on), and on an insufficient-balance error (recorded and reported). Output tokens dominate cost and an arm with no context may write long replies; the 4,096-token output limit is unchanged. One resubmission on a node fault with that node excluded; any other failure stops the arm and is reported.

**Analysis (exploratory, descriptive).** Resolved fraction, unusable and truncation rates, cost and tokens. Contrasts as estimates by the registered method (paired difference of the mean resolved fraction per instance, paired bootstrap interval, sign-flip permutation p): untrained minus floor and oracle minus floor, no adjustment, no decision. Stated with them: k = 1 here against 3 (untrained) and 2 (oracle), so each is also given at matched k (repeat 0 of the other arm); adjacent repeats are not independent runs; the minimum detectable difference from E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT. What the result shows and does not show is written after the run, whichever way it comes out.

**Code.** `ril/agent/providers/floor_arm.py`, `ril/analysis/rq3_floor.py`, `scripts/rq3_floor_arm.py`, `scripts/ada/rq3_floor_*.sbatch`.

**Design lines added while building** (none changes what is measured).
- *Prompts.* 377 prompts, 0 rejected by the screen, the text before the excerpt heading byte-identical to the oracle arm's on 377 of 377 instances; the excerpt section is the heading followed by nothing. The differential leak check ran on all 377 and reports 0 findings (vacuous by construction: no context). Prepare job 80737 (CPU, 6 s).
- *Path filter.* Items carry `paths: null` for this arm only (`UNRESTRICTED_PATH_ARMS`); the other arms' items are unchanged (tests).
- *Order and coverage.* The driver runs instances in sorted id order, so the 40 instances the arm covers are not a random draw of the 327: 13 astropy and 27 django (the 327 hold 13 astropy and 198 django: all 13 astropy and 27 of 198 django are covered, nothing from the other eight repositories).

### Results (exploratory; descriptive; nothing adjusted, nothing decided)

**Run.** Job 80738 (account A, 24 min), stopped `projected_cost` at the first instance boundary after 40 instances: 40 rows (1 repeat), 0 errors, 70 calls (30 follow-ups after unusable first replies), 1 unusable final reply (2.5%), 34 of 70 calls at the 4,096-token output limit (48.6%, against 13.2% for the oracle arm), mean 2,714 output tokens and 1,773 thought tokens per call (as logged), cache share 11%. Cost 0.786 USD for 40 instances; projected 6.43 USD for 327 against the 2.5 USD cap, so the arm stopped as specified and was not resumed or shortened to fit. Ledger and guard agree (0.786 USD).

**Resolved fraction (exact reading; interval = percentile bootstrap of the instance means; the tolerant reading gives the same counts).**

| Arm | Instances | Repeats | Resolved fraction | 95% interval |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Floor (no context) | 40 | 1 | 17.50% (7 of 40) | [7.50, 30.00] |
| Untrained rrf, same 40 | 40 | 3 | 30.00% | [16.67, 44.17] |
| Oracle, same 40 | 40 | 2 | 41.25% | [27.50, 56.25] |
| RAW, same 40 | 40 | 3 | 26.67% | [13.33, 40.83] |
| Untrained rrf, all 327 | 327 | 3 | 39.96% | [34.96, 45.06] |

Outcomes of the 40: resolved 7, applied but unresolved 4, apply failed 28 (no match 27, several matches 1), unusable 1. The seven resolved are astropy-13236, astropy-14309, django-11095, django-11099, django-11179, django-11292, django-11451.

**Contrasts (other arm minus floor; paired difference of the mean resolved fraction per instance, paired bootstrap interval, sign-flip permutation p; unadjusted; N = 40 paired instances).**

| Contrast | k form | Difference (points) | 95% interval | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Untrained minus floor | own k (3 vs 1) | +12.50 | [-0.83, +26.67] | 0.124 |
| Untrained minus floor | matched k (1 vs 1) | +12.50 | [-2.50, +27.50] | 0.175 |
| Oracle minus floor | own k (2 vs 1) | +23.75 | [+8.75, +38.75] | 0.0057 |
| Oracle minus floor | matched k (1 vs 1) | +27.50 | [+12.50, +42.50] | 0.0034 |

Four unadjusted estimates (eight rows with the tolerant reading, which is identical), no multiplicity claim and no decision.

**Minimum detectable difference.** The stored table of E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT is for N = 327 at a 40% rate: at k = 1 it is 8.18 points (assumed and measured agreement, kappa 1; blank in the calibrated scenario at kappa 4), at k = 2 7.12 to 7.91, at k = 3 6.79 to 7.59. It does not apply to 40 instances, where the detectable difference is larger; it was not recomputed. Caveats: k is 1 here against 2 and 3, and adjacent repeats are not independent runs, so the floor has no repeat-to-repeat noise estimate of its own (repeat SD not defined at k = 1).

**What this shows and does not show.** The stated expectation (far below the untrained arm, near zero) did not hold: with no retrieved code the model resolved 7 of 40 instances (17.5%, interval 7.5 to 30.0), and the difference to the untrained arm on the same 40 (+12.5 points) has an interval that includes zero at both k forms, so on this sample the floor is neither shown to be far below the untrained arm nor shown to be level with it. The oracle arm is above the floor by +23.75 to +27.5 points with intervals that exclude zero, so the harness can see a difference of that size between a context holding the gold files and no context; it could not, at 40 instances, see the 12.5-point difference between the untrained context and none. The arm does not show why edits applied without a context (the result is not explained here; reading it needs the replies and the issue texts, which were not examined), does not cover the 287 instances it never reached (the 40 are the first by sorted id: astropy and django only), and does not support any claim about the registered contrasts, which stay not distinguishable from zero. Absolute resolved rates are not capability claims. The positive-control purpose of the arm is met for the oracle contrast only.

**Spend.** 0.786 USD (guard and ledger equal; cap 2.5 USD, projection stop, never reached). Total **45.090 USD of 50; 4.910 USD of the 5.696 authorised remain** (the provider balance, about 4.9 USD by the lead's figure, cannot be read from here). Shared ledger: 33.525 committed against the 38.4 cap.

**Dead ends and incidents.** (1) Before the run: an empty context would have made every reply `path_not_in_context` (unusable); fixed by the arm-only `paths: null` above, covered by a test that shows both parser outcomes. (2) The projection stop ended the arm at 40 of 327; no cheaper variant (fewer instances chosen by a rule, a lower output limit, a different thinking level) was run, because the brief fixes the configuration and the cap. Not done: the remaining 287 instances (about 5.6 USD projected, above the remaining authorisation), a replay of the seven resolved replies against the issue texts, any arm added after seeing this one.

### Addendum 2026-11-15 (decision D170): completion of the floor arm, written before any further provider call

**Why.** Stage 1 stopped by its projected-cost rule after 40 of 327 instances (the first 40 by sorted id: 13 astropy, 27 of 198 django) and resolved 7 of 40 (17.5%), against a stated expectation of near zero. The lead decided to complete the arm as designed, so that the floor covers the whole confirmatory set. This remains an exploratory reference arm, outside the registered analysis. It is run to completion once; no further arm or stage is added afterwards.

**Completion run (paid, k = 1).**
- *Instances.* The 287 confirmatory instances not graded in stage 1; the held-out 50 are never graded. The arm is then 327 of 327. Stage 1 and the completion run are reported separately and pooled.
- *Unchanged.* No retrieved code, the same template, model and settings, the same reply handling, k = 1, one job on account A, the same executor environment. The configuration hash is read from the stored stage-1 `meta.json` and passed to the run, never typed; the run refuses to start on a mismatch.
- *Design line (tactical, no effect on what is measured).* The completion run writes to its own result directory with an instance list that excludes the 40 graded ones, so finished instances are never repeated and its call log, cost and projection belong to this stage alone. The stage-1 directory is read-only from here on. The alternative (resuming in the stage-1 directory) would count the stage-1 instances inside the first 60 of the projection and mix the two stages in one cost cap.
- *Spend.* The written authorisation (`total_cap_usd` 52, raised by the lead) is read before the run; the run refuses to start below 51. 45.090 USD is spent; this stage is capped at 6.2 USD. The projected cost of the 287 from stage 1 is about 5.7 USD (0.786 / 40 x 287 = 5.64), so no stop is taken on a projection below the cap; the projection is made again from the first 60 instances of this stage (cost of those 60 x 287 / 60) and the run stops cleanly only if it exceeds 6.2 USD. Stop at more than 10% unusable final replies (from 50 units on). On an insufficient-balance error the run stops, the number of completed instances is recorded and reported, and nothing is retried.
- *Shared ledger (tactical).* The existing shared ledger has a cap fixed at creation (38.4 USD, 33.525 committed), which cannot admit a further 6.2 USD. A copy of it is made with the committed amounts unchanged and the cap moved to 40.435 USD, which is 52 minus the 11.565 USD of earlier spend that the ledger does not hold (45.090 - 33.525), so the ledger cap and the written authorisation agree. The original file is kept untouched.
- *Node fault.* Resubmit once with that node excluded; any other failure stops the stage and is reported.

**No-call replay (descriptive).** From the stored replies of stage 1, and of the completion run once finished, with no provider call: for each resolved reply the repository, whether its quoted search text appears verbatim in the issue text, whether it appears in the base-commit file it edits, and how many of its edited lines are in the issue text; the same split for unresolved replies; resolved fraction by repository. A match with the base file is consistent with recall of the repository, or with the issue quoting the code; it does not establish memorisation. Issue texts are used only to compute these matches.

**Analysis (exploratory).** Over the full 327 and for each stage separately: resolved fraction with an interval, per repository, unusable and truncation rates, cost and tokens; contrasts against the untrained arm (k = 3) and the oracle arm (k = 2) by the registered paired method, own k and matched k, unadjusted, no decision; the minimum detectable difference of E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT at k = 1 and 327 instances. If the run stops short, only the completed instances are reported and nothing is extrapolated.

### Results of the completion run (addendum 2026-11-15, decision D170; exploratory; descriptive; nothing adjusted, nothing decided)

**The completion run stopped short.** Job 81110 (account A, 2 h 27 min, config hash `0fe87a17bf1b`, seed 0, results under `results/rq3_driver/floor_runs/gemini-3.8-flash/floor_stage2_runs0-0/`) graded **232 of the 287** planned instances and then stopped on an insufficient-balance error from the provider (`stopped: insufficient_balance`, 0 errors); nothing was retried, as specified. The 55 instances not run are the last in sorted id order and are all sympy (`results/rq3_floor/pooled/rq3_floor_instances.csv`). The arm therefore covers **272 of 327** confirmatory instances (stage 1: 40; completion run: 232), not 327 of 327, and no figure is extrapolated to the 55. The instances run are the first 272 by sorted id, not a random draw: all 198 django, all 13 astropy, 17 pytest, 16 xarray, 10 sympy, 9 sphinx, 3 requests, 3 pylint, 2 seaborn, 1 flask; the 327 hold 65 sympy, so sympy is 10 of 65 covered and every pooled figure is a reading of this subset. One further instance (sympy-13798) had a first reply logged and no final reply; it is not graded and its call (about 0.016 USD) is in the spend.

**Spend.** Stage 2: 4.132 USD by the guard and the ledger (equal), against the 6.2 USD cap; 60-instance projection 5.51 USD (below the cap, no stop). Whole arm 4.918 USD. Total **49.222 USD of 52 authorised** (45.090 before this stage). The provider balance cannot be read from here; the run stopped when it was exhausted. Shared-ledger copy: 37.657 committed against its 40.435 cap.

**Resolved fraction (exact reading; interval = percentile bootstrap of the instance means; the tolerant reading gives the same counts in both stages).**

| Set | Instances | Resolved | Fraction | 95% interval |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Stage 1 | 40 | 7 | 17.50% | [7.50, 30.00] |
| Completion run (partial) | 232 | 39 | 16.81% | [12.07, 21.98] |
| Pooled | 272 | 46 | 16.91% | [12.50, 21.32] |

The completion-run rate (16.81%) does not differ from stage 1 (17.50%): the difference is 0.7 points with intervals that overlap almost entirely. Per repository (pooled, resolved of instances): django 40 of 198 (20.2%), astropy 2 of 13, xarray 2 of 16, pytest 1 of 17, flask 1 of 1, and 0 of 10 sympy, 0 of 9 sphinx, 0 of 3 requests, 0 of 3 pylint, 0 of 2 seaborn (`results/rq3_floor/replay_all/rq3_floor_replay_by_repo.csv`); django is 73% of the instances, so the pooled rate is mostly the django rate and small repositories have counts too small to read.

Outcomes of the 232: resolved 39, applied but unresolved 20, apply failed 159, unusable 14 (6.0%; stage 1: 1 of 40, 2.5%; pooled 15 of 272, 5.5%, under the 10% stop). Calls at the 4,096-token output limit: 152 of 381 in the completion run (39.9%; stage 1 34 of 70, 48.6%); 17 of 232 final replies ended at the limit (stage 1: 6 of 40). Tokens, completion run: 379,363 input, 1,024,744 output of which 789,811 thought; stage 1: 110,017, 189,985 and 124,131.

**Contrasts (other arm minus floor; paired difference of the mean resolved fraction per instance, paired bootstrap interval, sign-flip permutation p; unadjusted; no decision).** Pooled, N = 272 paired instances (`results/rq3_floor/pooled/rq3_floor_contrasts.csv`; the sign-flip p is 0.0001 in all four rows, the smallest value 10,000 flips can give):

| Contrast | k form | Difference (points) | 95% interval |
|---|---|---|---|
| Untrained minus floor | own k (3 vs 1) | +25.00 | [+19.36, +30.51] |
| Untrained minus floor | matched k (1 vs 1) | +25.37 | [+19.49, +31.25] |
| Oracle minus floor | own k (2 vs 1) | +30.15 | [+23.90, +36.40] |
| Oracle minus floor | matched k (1 vs 1) | +29.78 | [+23.53, +36.03] |

Completion run alone (N = 232, `results/rq3_floor/stage2/`): untrained minus floor +27.16 [+21.12, +33.33] (matched k +27.59 [+21.12, +34.48]); oracle minus floor +31.25 [+24.57, +37.93] (matched k +30.17 [+23.28, +37.07]). Stage 1 alone is the table above (N = 40). On the 272 instances the untrained arm resolves 41.91% and the oracle arm 47.06%, against 16.91% for the floor (on the 232: 43.97%, 48.06%, 16.81%).

**Minimum detectable difference.** From E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT at N = 327 and a 40% rate, k = 1: 8.18 points (assumed and measured agreement, kappa 1; 9.75 at kappa 4; 7.88 in the calibrated scenario at kappa 1). The contrasts above are far larger than that, and N here is 272 rather than 327, so the bound is slightly optimistic; adjacent repeats are not independent runs and the floor has one repeat, so its repeat-to-repeat noise is not estimated.

**What this shows and does not show.** With no retrieved code the model resolved about 17% of the 272 instances run, in both stages alike. That is not near zero, so the stated expectation did not hold, and it is well below the arms with retrieved code (+25 points against the untrained retriever, +30 against the oracle), so the harness does see a large difference between a context with code and one without. The data cannot separate memorised knowledge of the repositories from information carried by the issue text: a floor of this size is consistent with recall of the repository, with issues that quote the code, or both. Absolute rates are not agent-capability claims. The subset is not a random draw (73% django), the 55 instances not run are not represented, k = 1 is set against 3 and 2, and the arm is exploratory and outside the registered analysis.

### No-call replay of the stored replies (descriptive; no provider call; jobs 81116 and 81425, account A, 4 and 14 minutes)

Code `ril/analysis/rq3_floor_replay.py`, `scripts/rq3_floor_replay.py`; tables under `results/rq3_floor/replay_all/` (stage 1 alone: `replay_s1/`, equal to the stage-1 rows of the pooled run). Each reply's edit blocks are matched against the issue text shown to the model and against the base-commit file the block edits; issue texts were used only to compute these matches. "In issue" is verbatim; a second count collapses runs of whitespace. Edited lines are the inserted or replaced lines of a block (stripped, non-blank).

| Replies (272) | Resolved (46) | Unresolved (226; 211 with edit blocks) |
|---|---|---|
| every SEARCH text in the issue, verbatim | 2 | 0 |
| same, whitespace collapsed | 8 | 6 |
| every SEARCH text in the base-commit file | 46 of 46 | 28 of 211 |
| at least one SEARCH text in the base file | 46 of 46 | 41 of 211 |
| edited lines found in the issue text | 57 of 155 (37%) | 90 of 830 (11%) |
| edited lines of 10 or more characters found in the issue | 39 of 125 | 64 of 732 |

By stage the pattern is the same (stage 1: resolved 7 of 7 with all SEARCH text in the base file, 2 verbatim in the issue; completion run: 39 of 39 and 0). So every resolved reply quoted code that exists in the base-commit file, and almost none quoted it from the issue verbatim; the unresolved replies mostly did not quote the base file correctly (159 of 232 completion-run instances fail to apply, 28 of 211 unresolved replies had all their SEARCH text in the file). A match with the base file is consistent with recall of the repository, or with the issue quoting the code in a form that differs from the verbatim text (8 of the 46 match once whitespace is collapsed); it does not establish memorisation, and the replay has no comparison set of code the model could not have seen. 57 of 155 edited lines of resolved replies also appear in the issue, which is what an issue that states the fix would give.

### Incidents and dead ends (completion run)

(1) Insufficient balance at 232 of 287: recorded, not retried; the brief's rule is to report what completed. (2) The shared ledger's cap could not admit 6.2 USD; a copy with the cap moved to 40.435 USD (52 minus the 11.565 USD held outside the ledger) was used and the original left untouched. (3) The first draft of the analysis script took one directory per arm; the pooled and stage-2 tables are built from a concatenation of the two stages' stored rows (pooled copy kept outside the repository; the stage directories are the sources). Not done: the 55 remaining instances (about 1.0 USD at the observed 0.0178 USD per instance) because the balance is exhausted; any further arm or stage, by the rule stated above.

## E-REPORT-FINAL: final report pass, RQ3 results, abstract, contributions, conclusion, last proofread. Writing, 2026-11-14 (decision D168)

Status: **done (addendum 2026-11-15 fills the two oracle slots; 28 pages, 0 open slots, no overfull box, no undefined reference; the first pass left one marked slot each in the abstract and the RQ3 section for E-RQ3-ORACLE by the lead's ruling).** No model call; one CPU cluster job in the addendum. Evidence class: measured (every number of the new text is read from the stored output of E-RQ3-AGENT, by a generator or by a comment naming the entry and file).

**Gate.** `E-RQ3-AGENT` was on main (merge `9d167fe`) and its stored inputs verified: the SHA-256 of the arms table, the contrasts table, and the result and call files of the three trained arms equal the values in `results/rq3_driver/remaining_arms/arm_summary.json`; the untrained arm's two files and the plan, subset, held-out list and ledger inputs of that summary were not re-hashed in this pass.

**Slots filled.** (1) Results of the graded runs: Table `rq3_agent` (resolved of 981, mean fraction with bootstrap interval, per repeat, any-of-three, unusable replies, calls at the 4,096-token limit, cost) and Table `rq3_agent_contrasts` (primary RAW minus untrained +2.45 points, interval -0.82 to +5.91, sign-flip p 0.177; five estimates; any-of-three beside them), with the minimum detectable difference of E-RQ3-RUN-AGREEMENT (7.3 to 9.4 points at the measured 40% rate, 4.9 to 9.0 at 10 to 30%) written next to every non-rejection, and the statement that adjacent cached repeats are not independent runs. (2) Threats added by the arm comparison (one model and provider, thinking low, output limit with per-arm truncation 13.7 to 15.5%, one-shot repair, one environment, run-to-run spread 0.53 to 1.16 points, contamination exposure, the GPU caveat on the trained models, one training seed per arm). (3) Abstract, contribution, conclusion, status sentences of the introduction and of the RQ3 section, threats rows T16 and T17. (4) Token and cost: Table `rq3_budget`; text states 38.587 of 50 USD spent, 11.413 remaining (the model check's 1.690 USD is quoted from E-RQ3-MODEL-CHECK-RESULT because its superseded-configuration and cache-probe logs are not stored, so the table sums the four arms, 36.897 USD).

**Found while reading the sources.** (a) The untrained arm was graded entirely on 2026-11-13; the call logs of the trained arms carry 2026-11-13 for 268 of 1,230 (RAW) and 266 of 1,236 (FILTER-BM25) calls and 2026-11-14 for the rest, the subsample entirely on 2026-11-14, so the primary contrast compares runs on different days of a hosted model; the report says so as a threat. (b) The earlier report text counted 29 of 981 final replies of the untrained arm at the output limit; the call-level count is 201 of 1,294 (15.5%) and 29 final replies is a different count that the entry E-RQ3-AGENT does not repeat; both stay as they were, labelled by what they count (calls, final replies). (c) E-RQ3-AGENT says one of five estimates excludes zero; the contrast table has six rows, the primary and five estimates, so the statement is correct. (d) The retrieval-versus-reasoning breakdown of failures is not made by the entry and not made here; the report says it is open.

**Cut because no record supports it.** A per-repository reading of the agent runs and a statement about the cause of the subsample-below-RAW estimate were not written; the entry reads that estimate as not a finding.

**Decision D168 (tactical, a reading, not a design change).** The report keeps exactly one open slot per place for the oracle arm, as the lead asked, so `check_report.py` reports 2 open slots, not 0 (the prompt's zero was written before the oracle arm existed); `tests/unit/test_report_tables.py` lists `E-RQ3-ORACLE` in `PENDING_ENTRIES`, so its registry row may be missing until the entry is made, and the id is removed from that set when the slot is filled. Table evidence classes of the three new tables are added to the test's list of measured, non-exploratory fragments.

**Not done.** The oracle slots; the retrieval-versus-reasoning breakdown; a rerun of the power table with the discordance of the graded arms; WEIGHT, CURRICULUM, other seeds, the SweRank cell (the entry's list).

**Dead ends.** The first table build failed its digit rule (the title said RQ3); the first widths of the three new tables overflowed (12 boxes) and were narrowed; a comment naming a `.jsonl` call log is not read by `check_report.py` (file not found), so the date claim cites the entries instead.

Files: `reports/final/final_report.tex`, `reports/final/generated/{rq3_agent,rq3_agent_contrasts,rq3_budget}.tex` and `SOURCES.csv`, `ril/analysis/report_tables.py`, `tests/unit/test_report_tables.py`, `docs/REPORT_GAPS.md`.

**Addendum 2026-11-15 (D168): oracle slots filled, leak check over every arm, hash typo, formatting.** Evidence class: measured (stored tables of E-RQ3-ORACLE; one CPU job, 6 min 49 s).
- *Slots.* The abstract, the RQ3 section and the conclusion now state the oracle calibration arm from `results/rq3_oracle/`: 45.11% against 39.96% untrained (+5.15 points [+0.61, +9.73], unadjusted p 0.035, one of eight comparisons, below the minimum detectable 7.1 to 7.9 points at two repeats, so probably inflated) and +2.70 over RAW (not distinguishable from zero); gold files cut or partly left out in 208 of 327 (a calibration, not a ceiling); descriptive exploratory breakdown on the 37 instances where the untrained and RAW contexts held no gold file (3.6% and 5.4% against 28.4%); the reading that the scaffold appears limited by reasoning more than by retrieval is labelled a reading, not a proven cause, and the arm is not presented as evidence of what any trained retriever would gain. Two generated tables (`rq3_oracle`, `rq3_oracle_gap`), exploratory in their evidence class. The conclusion no longer says that the retrieval-versus-reasoning breakdown remains to be made; it says that a breakdown of failure causes beyond the descriptive oracle view was not made.
- *Rounding.* The report table shows the oracle-minus-RAW lower bound as -1.99 (stored -0.019877); the entry of E-RQ3-ORACLE and the brief write -1.98. The stored value rounds to -1.99; the entry text is left as recorded.
- *Differential leak check over the other arms (job 80706, account A).* The oracle gate's check (gold or test patch text counts only when absent from the base-commit file whose block holds it, every context line from that file) run over the prompts of RAW, FILTER-BM25 and the subsample: 0 findings in 377 prompts each (pre-existing matches 460, 449 and 468; reports with digests only in `results/rq3_oracle/leak_mined_*_u100_s100.json`). With the oracle (0 in 377) and untrained (0 in 1,131 prompt items) results of the oracle gate, no arm has a finding. A tripwire on a pre-patch snapshot, not a proof about the model. Job script `scripts/ada/rq3_arms_leakgate.sbatch`.
- *Configuration hash.* The mistyped string (wrong last character) is removed from the entries, the state file, the job log and the story; the recorded value starts 0fe87a17bf1b. The incident is kept, described without the wrong string.
- *Formatting.* Three line-length errors of `reports/mid/make_figures.py` fixed (a docstring and one call wrapped); no behaviour change.

**Addendum 2026-11-15 (second, D168): RQ1 in the abstract and contributions, conclusion rephrased.** Evidence class: measured (stored files of E-RQ1-LADDER and E-FREEZE-AND-LATENCY; no run, no cluster job).
- *RQ1 in the abstract and the contributions list,* exploratory on the development repositories as the body states it: BM25 by rank fusion on the dense retriever raises Recall@100 at both query levels (+8.9 at Level 2, interval [2.4, 15.4], repository-clustered; +9.0 at Level 3, query-level), same-file expansion raises it further at Level 3 (+12.2, query-level over two repositories), the typed graph does not beat the same-file control (Recall@100 -5.6 at Level 3, below the control; nDCG@10 -0.5 at Level 2, not distinguishable from zero). Per-stage median cost: fusion 5.04 ms, same-file expansion 0.41 ms, the stored-graph row 1.14 ms (it includes a BM25 pass; 0.46 ms for the expansion alone), dense path 18.34 ms, timed on different machines (GPU node for the dense stages, laptop CPU for the rest), not an end-to-end percentile. The same-file gain at Level 2 (Recall@100 +15.95, repository-clustered) is also in the stored table; the abstract states Level 3 only, as the lead asked, and the body keeps the nDCG@10 reading at Level 2.
- *Conclusion.* "What is reported so far is" became "This report presents"; the first paragraph now separates the exploratory apparatus results (including the ladder) from the confirmatory family (the pre-registered RQ2 comparison and the registered RQ3 contrasts), where before it said both that no result was confirmatory and that the confirmatory question was answered.

## E-REPORT-SHORT: the two-page report, written from the verified full report. Writing, 2026-11-15 (decision D171)

Status: **done (2 pages by `pdfinfo`, 10 pt body, 0.7 in margins, no overfull box, no undefined reference).** No model call, no cluster job. Evidence class: measured (every number is read from a stored file by a generator or by a comment on its own line naming the entry and the file, and every number also stands in the full report).

**What was built.** `reports/short/short_report.tex` (same class, fonts, margins and two-column structure as the mid-term and full reports); two compact tables through `ril/analysis/short_report_tables.py` and `scripts/short_report_tables.py` into `reports/short/generated/` (the RQ1 ladder with p50, p95 and p99 of every added stage at the django index size; the RQ3 arms with k and the number of instances, the oracle and floor rows marked exploratory), read from the same stored tables as the full report's own fragments; the build `make report-short` (`ril/util/short_report_build.py`: regenerates the tables, refuses a body below 10 pt, margins below 0.7 in or a changed line spacing, typesets, fails on an overfull box or an unresolved reference, and fails unless `pdfinfo` reports exactly two pages); the PDF is in `.gitignore` like the full report's.

**Checks added.** `scripts/check_report.py --short` (`ril/util/report_check.py`): a line with a number needs its own `%` comment (stricter than the full report's rule, which lets one comment close the lines above it); the named stored files must exist; every number of the short report and its tables must stand in the full report or its generated tables, with the same value or as a rounding of a value with more decimals (a table printed at one decimal where the full report prints two). Tests in `tests/unit/test_short_report.py`: the fragments equal a fresh build, a typed digit is refused, a missing same-line comment is refused, an invented number is refused, the build rules refuse an overfull box, a third page, smaller type or smaller margins; the page-count test runs when the PDF is built.

**What was cut from the full report, and why.** Background, related work, the data and system sections, the evaluation setup, the label audit, contamination, the injected-noise and mined-set pilots, the protocol items O1 to O15, the second power table, the model check, tokens and cost, the 30-row threats table (reduced to one paragraph of the limitations the brief names), reproducibility, the recency prior, the unit-level BM25 readings, graph recovery and storage, the any-of-three readings, the oracle's retrieval-versus-reasoning breakdown and the floor's replay: none is needed to state the question, the method, the three results and what they allow; the full report keeps all of it. Type size and margins were not touched (the first build already fitted two pages at 10 pt with small-type tables).

**Wording kept from the full report.** "Matched optimization budgets"; exploratory against confirmatory labelled in every section and caption; the null stated with its detectable difference ("about 7 to 9 points"), not as no effect; the oracle estimate "probably inflated" and a calibration, not a ceiling; the floor arm's reading limited to what the data cannot separate; the floor covering 272 of 327 instances. The corrected interval field (DEV-044) is one clause in the method; the full report states the corrected intervals and cites the deviation only in a comment, so the clause is the one place where the short report names a record the full report names less directly (it adds no number).

**Decision D171 (tactical).** Single author line as the brief fixes (the full and mid-term reports list three authors); no repository link or placeholder in the text (the lead can add the link); table type `\small`, as the full report's tables; the agent model is not named (one hosted model from one provider); the consistency rule accepts a rounding of a more precise value, because the two compact tables print one decimal where the full report prints two in prose.

**Found while building.** The first build fitted two pages with about a tenth of the second page free; the first table widths overflowed (padding of the five columns was not counted) and were narrowed; the digit rule of the table class refused "p50 / p95 / p99" in a caption (percentile names, registered as vocabulary); the first draft of the RQ2 sentence on the excluded gain said the intervals against both comparators exclude gains above 0.70 to 1.22 points, which is false for FILTER against RAW on the executable subset (upper end 1.57), and was rewritten to the four stored bounds with their comparators.

**Not done.** The repository link (for the lead); a Verified reading of the RQ1 ladder (as in the full report).

Files: `reports/short/short_report.tex`, `reports/short/generated/{short_rq1,short_rq3}.tex` and `SOURCES.csv`, `ril/analysis/short_report_tables.py`, `ril/util/{short_report_build,report_check}.py`, `scripts/{short_report_tables,build_short_report,check_report}.py`, `tests/unit/test_short_report.py`, `Makefile`, `.gitignore`, `docs/REPORT_GAPS.md`.
